is applicable for
L_{\eta}
and, therefore, we may use the results from Section
2
to compute the
A_{\infty}
-algebra of interest.

Returning to the calculation of
CM_{-*}(\Omega(S^{2},\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}))
, we put the points
\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime},\bm{q}^{\prime\prime}
etc. in position to satisfy
1
–
3
and the following:

(1)

\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime},\bm{q}^{\prime\prime}
etc. are contained in a small ball with respect to the standard round metric;

(2)

the
r
-coordinates of
q_{1},q_{2},\dots
are in decreasing order; and

(3)

the
\phi
-coordinates of
q_{1},q_{1}^{\prime},q_{1}^{\prime\prime},\dots
are in decreasing order.

We claim that computations that we previously performed in the case of round metric yield the same results for the Lagrangian
L_{\eta}
. The main reason for this is that for generators in degrees
0
and
1
the Finsler (pseudo-)gradient trajectories behave similarly to the round metric case.

Let
A_{\kappa,F}
denote the HDHF
A_{\infty}
-algebra of
\kappa
cotangent fibers and let
A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta}
denote the Morse multipath
A_{\infty}
-algebra corresponding to
L_{\eta}
as above. We will use the notation
\tau_{\geq-M}(A)
to denote the
(-M)
-truncated subcomplex obtained from
A
by setting all elements of degree less than
-M
equal to
0
. The quasi-equivalence between
A_{\kappa,F}
and
A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta}
given by Theorem
3.11
induces a quasi-isomorphism between the truncations
\tau_{\geq-M}(A_{\kappa,F})
and
\tau_{\geq-M}(A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta})
.

Claim 4.12
.

For
M=\big\lfloor\frac{2(1-\varepsilon)}{1-\lambda}\big\rfloor
and
d\geq 3
we have:

\mu_{M}^{d}(\bm{\gamma}_{d},\ldots,\bm{\gamma}_{1})=0\text{, whenever }|\gamma_{1}|+\ldots+|\gamma_{d}|+(2-d)>-M.

In particular, the truncated complex
\tau_{\geq-\lfloor\frac{M}{3}\rfloor+1}(A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta})
has an induced structure of a dga.

Proof.

Arguing by contradiction, suppose there exist multipaths
\bm{\gamma}_{1},\dots,\bm{\gamma}_{d}
in
\tau_{\geq-M}(A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta})
with
d\geq 3
such that
m_{d}(\bm{\gamma}_{1},\dots,\bm{\gamma}_{d})\neq 0
. In particular there is an MFTS connecting
(\bm{\gamma}_{1},\dots,\bm{\gamma}_{d})
to some
\bm{\gamma}_{0}
, and we have

\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}_{0})=\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}_{1})+\dots+\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}_{d})+(d-2)<\big\lfloor\tfrac{2(m-\varepsilon)}{1-\lambda}\big\rfloor.

By Lemma
4.10
, we have:

(4.16)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{\sqrt{L_{\eta}}}(\bm{\gamma}_{0})\approx\frac{2\pi\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}_{0})}{1+\lambda}=\frac{2\pi(\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}_{1})+\dots+\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}_{d})+(d-2))}{1+\lambda},

(4.17)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{\sqrt{L_{\eta}}}(\bm{\gamma}_{1})+\dots+\mathscr{A}_{\sqrt{L_{\eta}}}(\bm{\gamma}_{d})\approx\frac{2\pi(\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}_{1})+\dots+\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}_{d}))}{1+\lambda},

where the estimate is up to some terms involving the round metric distances among
\bm{q}
,
\bm{q}^{\prime}
,
\bm{q}^{\prime\prime}
etc., and the terms from Lemma
4.10
, which can all be taken to be small.
Since the
\mathscr{A}_{\sqrt{L_{\eta}}}
-action is decreasing along the pseudogradient trajectories for
\mathscr{A}_{L_{\eta}}
, we obtain a contradiction since
d-2\geq 1
by comparing (
4.16
) and (
4.17
). The first part of the claim then follows. To show the latter, observe that
\mu^{3}_{M}
vanishes on
\tau_{\geq-\lfloor\frac{M}{3}\rfloor+1}(A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta})
and therefore the tuple
(\tau_{\geq-\lfloor\frac{M}{3}\rfloor+1}(A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta}),\mu^{1}_{M},\mu^{2}_{M})
is a dga.
∎

Claim 4.13
.

As an algebra over
\mathbb{Z}\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket
, the dga
\tau_{\geq-\lfloor\frac{M}{3}\rfloor+1}(A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta})
is generated by the elements
T_{1},\dots,T_{\kappa-1}
and
x_{1},\dots,x_{\kappa}
.

Proof of Claim
4.13
.

Using the fact that
A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta}
is generated by
\kappa
-tuples of geodesics from
{\bm{q}}
to
{\bm{q}}^{\prime}
, the algebra structure of
\tau_{\geq-\lfloor\frac{M}{3}\rfloor+1}(A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta})
for
\kappa=1
, and the relations (
1.3
)-(
1.7
) and (
4.6
), any
\kappa
-tuple
\bm{\gamma}_{0}
can be written as
\pm x_{1}^{j_{1}}\dots x_{\kappa}^{j_{\kappa}}T_{\sigma}
for some
\sigma\in S_{\kappa}
, modulo
\hbar
terms. We then iterate this procedure to eventually write
\bm{\gamma}_{0}
as a possibly infinite linear combination of terms of the form
\pm\hbar^{i}x_{1}^{j_{1}}\dots x_{\kappa}^{j_{\kappa}}T_{\sigma}
.
∎

Remark 4.14
.

The sums are expected to be finite, but the energy functional does not seem to give us sufficiently good control.

4.4.
Completion of proof of Theorem
1.7

We now complete the proof of Theorem
1.7
.

No other relations.
We claim that in each of the algebras
\tau_{\geq-M}(A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta})
there are no other relations besides those that are in the ideal
\mathscr{I}
generated by (
1.3
)–(
1.7
). Suppose we have a relation

(4.18)

R_{0}\coloneqq w_{0}+\hbar w_{1}+\dots+\hbar^{k}w_{k}=0.

of fixed degree; each
w_{i}
is a linear combination of finitely many monomials and
w_{0}\neq 0
. Moreover, by Lemma
4.5
we may assume that each
w_{i}
is a linear combination of monomials
x^{\alpha}T_{\sigma}
of PBW type. Now each monomial
x^{\alpha}T_{\sigma}
has a representative of the form
\sum_{i\geq 0}(\sum_{j}a_{ij}\bm{\gamma}_{ij})\hbar^{i}
, where each
\bm{\gamma}_{ij}
is a tuple of geodesics and the leading term
\sum_{j}a_{0j}\bm{\gamma}_{0j}
is a tuple of geodesics
x^{\alpha_{k}}
of permutation type
\sigma
. After expressing
R_{0}
via the above geodesic representatives, the terms with
\hbar^{0}
all come from monomials of
w_{0}
, and all are represented by different tuples
\bm{\gamma}_{j}
, unless
w_{0}=0
. This is a contradiction.

The truncations
\tau_{\geq-\lfloor\frac{M}{3}\rfloor}(A_{\kappa,\varepsilon,\eta})
and
\tau_{\geq-\lfloor\frac{M}{3}\rfloor}(H_{\kappa})
therefore coincide. As a corollary the two
A_{\infty}
-algebras have the same cohomology rings. Hence by taking
M\to\infty
the above argument already shows that the dga
H_{\kappa}
has the same cohomology as
A_{\kappa,F}
.

Quasi-equivalence between
A_{\kappa,F}
and
H_{\kappa}
.

Fix sequences
(N_{i}=i)_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
and
(\lambda_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
as in Lemma
4.10
and denote by
(A_{i},\{\mu^{d}_{i}\}_{d\geq 1})
the
A_{\infty}
-algebra
A_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\lambda_{i}),\eta(\lambda_{i})}
constructed above. As graded modules we can decompose
A_{i}=\bigoplus_{s\leq 0}A_{i}(s)
, where
A_{i}(s)
is the degree
s
part.
Note that each
A_{i}
is non-positively graded. Claim
4.12
then implies that the following property holds.

(1)

For any
s\leq 0
, the
A_{\infty}
-multiplication
\mu_{i}^{d}:A_{i}^{\otimes d}(s)\to A_{i}(s-d+2)
is zero for
i\geq-s+d-2
, where
d\geq 3
.

There is a homotopy between
L_{\eta_{i}}
and
L_{\eta_{i+1}}
which arises from varying
\lambda
from
\lambda_{i}
to
\lambda_{i+1}
. Counting
two-colored Morse flow trees with switches
corresponding to such homotopy as in
[
Maz21
,
Maz21a
]
introduces an
A_{\infty}
-morphism
\mathscr{F}_{i}\colon A_{i}\to A_{i+1}
for each
i\geq 1
. We use without proof the fact that all the
\mathscr{F}_{i}
are quasi-equivalances and refer the reader to
[
Maz21
, Proposition 20]
for a detailed account of this claim in the finite-dimensional setting. A rigorous proof in our setting can be carried out in a similar manner by introducing moduli spaces of two-colored trees with switches.

Note that each
\mathscr{F}_{i}^{1}|_{\hbar=0}
is an identity morphism, since for
\kappa=1
in each degree both
A_{i}
and
A_{i+1}
have a unique generator (which is also a cycle) and
\mathscr{F}_{i}^{1}|_{\hbar=0}
is a quasi-isomorphism. Since we are working over
\mathbb{Z}\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket
, it follows that
\mathscr{F}_{i}^{1}
is an isomorphism. Therefore,

(1)

For any
s\leq 0
, the map
\mathscr{F}_{i}^{1}:A_{i-1}(s)\to A_{i}(s)
is an isomorphism of abelian groups for
i\geq-s
.

Finally, using action arguments as in the proof of Claim
4.12
one may show that
\mathscr{F}_{i}^{k}(\bm{\gamma}_{k},\ldots,\bm{\gamma}_{1})=0
whenever
k\geq 2
and
|\bm{\gamma}_{k}|+\dots+|\bm{\gamma}_{1}|\geq k-1-i
. Hence the
A_{\infty}
-morphisms
\mathscr{F}_{i}
also satisfy:

(1)

For any
s\leq 0
, the map
\mathscr{F}_{i}^{k}:A_{i-1}^{\otimes k}(s)\to A_{i}(s-k+1)
is zero for
i\geq-s+k-1
, where
k>1
.

Roughly speaking, Properties (A)–(C) say that, for a fixed degree
s
, the
A_{\infty}
-morphism
\mathscr{F}_{i}
is the identity map for
i
sufficiently large.

We use Properties (A)–(C) to show that the direct limit
A
of the system
(\mathscr{F}_{i})_{i,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}}
exists in Lemma
4.15
. The limit
A
is quasi-isomorphic to each
A_{i}
. Finally, we show in Lemma
4.16
that
A
is isomorphic to the dga
H_{\kappa}
. This completes the proof of Theorem
1.7
.

4.5.
A direct limit argument

Lemma 4.15
.

Suppose
(A_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
and
(\mathscr{F}_{i}^{k}:A_{i-1}^{\otimes k}\to A_{i})_{i,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}}
are sequences of non-positively graded
A_{\infty}
-algebras and
A_{\infty}
-morphisms such that Properties (A)–(C) above hold.
Then the direct limit of the system
(\mathscr{F}_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}}
exists. Moreover, such a limit can be explicitly described from
A_{i}
and it is a dga.

Proof.

The proof is carried out in three steps:

(1)

Define an
A_{\infty}
-algebra
A=\oplus_{s}A(s)
.

(2)

Construct a family of
A_{\infty}
-morphisms
\mathscr{G}_{i}=(\mathscr{G}_{i}^{k})
, where
\mathscr{G}_{i}^{k}:A_{i}^{\otimes k}\to A
, and check that
\mathscr{G}_{i+1}\circ\mathscr{F}_{i+1}=\mathscr{F}_{i}
.

(3)

Verify that
A
satisfies the universal property of a direct limit.

Step 1.
Define
A=\oplus_{s\leq 0}A(s)
and
A(s)=\varinjlim A_{i}(s)
, where we consider the directed system of
\mathbb{Z}\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket
-modules associated with
\mathscr{F}_{i}^{1}:A_{i-1}(s)\to A_{i}(s)
.
By Property (B) there exist
\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1}:A_{i}(s)\to A(s)
such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{A_{i-1}(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{F}_{i}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{i-1}^{1}}
\textstyle{A_{i}(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1}}
\textstyle{A(s),}

where all the maps are isomorphisms for
i\geq-s
.

We now define the
A_{\infty}
-operations
(\mu^{d})_{d\geq 1}
on
A
. For any
s\leq 0
,
\mu^{1}
is defined by the following diagram:

\textstyle{A(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{1}}
\textstyle{A(s+1)}
\textstyle{A_{i}(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{i}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{F}_{i+1}^{1}}
\textstyle{A_{i}(s+1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{F}_{i+1}^{1}}
\textstyle{A_{i+1}(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{i+1}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{i+1}^{1}}
\textstyle{A_{i+1}(s+1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{i+1}^{1}}

for
i\geq-s
. Here all vertical maps are isomorphisms. The map
\mu^{1}
is independent of the choice of
i
since the lower square is commutative.

The map
\mu^{2}
is defined via the following diagram:

\textstyle{A(s)\otimes A(t)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{2}}
\textstyle{A(s+t)}
\textstyle{A_{i}(s)\otimes A_{i}(t)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{2}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1}}
\textstyle{A_{i}(s+t)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1}}

for
i\geq-s-t
.
One can similarly check that
\mu^{2}
is independent of the choice of
i
as in the case of
\mu^{1}
.
The corresponding square is commutative by Properties (B) and (C).

The map
\mu^{3}
is defined by the following diagram:

\textstyle{A(s)\otimes A(t)\otimes A(r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{3}}
\textstyle{A(s+t+r)}
\textstyle{A_{i}(s)\otimes A_{i}(t)\otimes A_{i}(r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{i}^{3}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1}}
\textstyle{A_{i}(s+t+r-1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1}}

for
i\geq-s-t-r+1
. It is independent of choices of
i
. Moreover,
\mu_{i}^{3}
is zero by Property (A). It follows that
\mu^{3}
is the zero map.
The maps
\mu^{k}
,
k\geq 3
, can be defined similarly and are zero maps.

To verify that the collection
(\mu^{d})_{d\geq 1}
satisfies the
A_{\infty}
-relations, observe that for any given generators as inputs, we can choose
i\gg 0
such that
(\mu^{d})_{d\geq 1}
satisfies the
A_{\infty}
-relations for these generators if and only if
(\mu_{i}^{d})_{d\geq 1}
does for the corresponding generators in
A_{i}
. The latter is true since
A_{i}
is an
A_{\infty}
-algebra.

Step 2.
We construct a family of
A_{\infty}
-morphisms
\mathscr{G}_{i}=(\mathscr{G}_{i}^{k})_{k\geq 1}
, where
\mathscr{G}_{i}^{k}:A_{i}^{\otimes k}\to A
is defined by the following diagram:

\textstyle{A_{i}^{\otimes k}(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{i}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{(\mathscr{F}_{m}\circ\cdots\circ\mathscr{F}_{i+1})^{k}\hskip 8.19447pt\,}
\textstyle{A(s-k+1)}
\textstyle{A_{m}(s-k+1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{G}_{m}^{1}}

for
m\geq\mbox{max}\{i,-s+k-1\}
.
Here
(\mathscr{F}_{m}\circ\cdots\circ\mathscr{F}_{i+1})^{k}
denotes the degree
1-k
part of the composition
\mathscr{F}_{m}\circ\cdots\circ\mathscr{F}_{i+1}
of
A_{\infty}
-morphisms.
It is straightforward to verify that
\mathscr{G}_{i}=(\mathscr{G}_{i}^{k})_{k\geq 1}
is an
A_{\infty}
-morphism. Roughly speaking,
\mathscr{G}_{i}=\mathscr{F}_{m}\circ\cdots\circ\mathscr{F}_{i+1}
for sufficiently large
m
.
It follows that

(4.19)

\mathscr{G}_{i+1}\circ\mathscr{F}_{i+1}=\mathscr{G}_{i}.

Step 3.

We verify that
A
satisfies the universal property of the direct limit.
Suppose that there is an
A_{\infty}
-algebra
B
and a family of
A_{\infty}
-morphisms
\mathscr{H}_{i}=(\mathscr{H}_{i}^{k})_{k\geq 1}
, where
\mathscr{H}_{i}^{k}:A_{i}^{\otimes k}\to B
, such that
\mathscr{H}_{i}=\mathscr{H}_{i+1}\circ\mathscr{F}_{i+1}
.
We construct an
A_{\infty}
-morphism
\mathscr{H}=(\mathscr{H}^{k})_{k\geq 1}
, where
\mathscr{H}^{k}:A^{\otimes k}\to B
is defined by the following diagram:

\textstyle{A^{\otimes k}(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{H}^{k}}
\textstyle{B(s-k+1)}
\textstyle{A_{i}^{\otimes k}(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathscr{G}_{i}^{1})^{\otimes k}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{H}_{i}^{k}}

for
i\geq-s+k-1
. It is easy to verify that
\mathscr{H}
is an
A_{\infty}
-morphism.
Moreover,
\mathscr{H}\circ\mathscr{G}_{m}=\mathscr{H}_{m}
for sufficiently large
m
. Hence
\mathscr{H}\circ\mathscr{G}_{i}=\mathscr{H}_{i}
holds for any
i
as we may proceed by induction on
i
using (
4.19
):

\mathscr{H}\circ\mathscr{G}_{i}=\mathscr{H}\circ\mathscr{G}_{i+1}\circ\mathscr{F}_{i+1}=\mathscr{H}_{i+1}\circ\mathscr{F}_{i+1}=\mathscr{H}_{i}.

∎

The limit
A
constructed above is a dga since
\mu^{k}=0
for
k\geq 3
.

Lemma 4.16
.

Specializing to the case of
A_{i}=A_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\lambda_{i}),\eta(\lambda_{i})}
, the direct limit dga
A
is isomorphic to
H_{\kappa}
.

Proof.

The dga
H_{\kappa}
is generated by the
T_{i}
and
x_{1}
.
There are corresponding elements in each
A_{i}
, hence in
A
.
By abuse of notation, these elements still denoted by
T_{i}
and
x_{1}
.
We then define a map
\phi:H_{\kappa}\to A
which sends
T_{i}
to
T_{i}
and
x_{1}
to
x_{1}
.
The map
\phi
extends to a dga morphism since all the relations of
H_{\kappa}
are preserved by
\phi
.
By Claim
4.13
, each
A_{i}
above a certain degree is generated by the
T_{i}
and
x_{1}
, which clearly implies that
A
is generated by the
T_{i}
and
x_{1}
. Hence the morphism
\phi
is surjective; it is also injective since
\phi|_{\hbar=0}
is the identity.
∎

Appendix A
Regularity for unstable manifolds

The goal of this appendix is to prove Theorem
A.10
. We learned the main ideas of the proof from Alberto Abbondandolo, to whom we are grateful.

A.1.
Preliminaries on difference quotients

Let
S^{1}=[0,1]/(0\sim 1)
. Given
f:S^{1}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
and a real number
h>0
, the difference quotient
D^{h}f
is given by:

D^{h}f(x)=\frac{f(x+h)-f(x)}{h}.

The following is immediate from
[
Wen08
, Theorem A.1.22]
; similar statements are standard and can be found for example in
[
Eva98
, Section 5.8.2]
.

Lemma A.1
.

Assume
1\leq p<\infty
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

(1)

If
f\in W^{k,p}(S^{1},\mathbb{R}^{n})
, then
D^{h}f
converges to
f^{\prime}
in
W^{k-1,p}(S^{1},\mathbb{R}^{n})
as
h\to 0
.

(2)

Suppose
p>1
. If
f\in W^{k-1,p}(S^{1},\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and
\|D^{h}f\|_{L^{p}}\leq C
for some
C>0
and all
h>0
in a neighborhood of
0\in\mathbb{R}
, then
f\in W^{k,p}(S^{1},\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Corollary A.2
.

The following statements are equivalent for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
:

(1)

The
L^{2}
-map
\gamma\colon S^{1}\to M
represents an element in
W^{k,2}(S^{1},M)
.

(2)

The path
T_{\tau}(\gamma)_{\tau}\colon(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\to W^{k-1,2}(S^{1},M)
,
\tau\mapsto(t\mapsto\gamma(\tau+t))
,
is differentiable at
\tau=0
with respect to the smooth structure on
W^{k-1,2}(S^{1},M)
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
A.1
since the derivative of the path
T_{\tau}(\gamma)
at
\tau=0
is equal to the limit of the difference quotients in the appropriate local chart of
W^{k,2}(S^{1},\gamma^{*}TM)
, as in
[
Kli12
]
.
∎

The goal of the remainder of this subappendix is to prove the analogs of the above results in the non-periodic setting using
twisted difference quotients
. Let
T\colon(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})_{h}\times[0,1]_{x}\to[0,1]
be a smooth function satisfying the following:

\displaystyle T_{0}\equiv 0,T_{h}(0)=0,T_{h}(1)=0\mbox{ for all $h$};

(A.1)

\displaystyle(\partial_{h}T_{h})|_{h=0}(x)>0\text{ away from $x=0,1$};

\displaystyle\partial_{x}T_{h}(x)>-1\mbox{ for all $h$},

where we are writing
T_{h}(x)=T(h,x).

Example A.3
.

Choose
\delta>0
small and
\varepsilon_{T}
satisfying
0<\varepsilon_{T}<\delta
. We set
T_{h}(x)=h
for
x\in[\delta,1-\delta]
,
T_{h}(x)=\frac{h}{\delta}x
for
x\in[0,\delta]
, and
T_{h}(x)=\frac{h}{\delta}(1-x)
for
x\in[1-\delta,1]
, and then smooth out this family near the corners.

\delta
(1-\delta)
1
\delta+h
(1-\delta)+h
1
x
A_{h}(x)

Figure A.1
.
The graph of
A_{h}(x)=\operatorname{id}+T_{h}(x)
before smoothing the corners.

By (
A.1
),
A_{h}=\operatorname{id}+T_{h}
,
h\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})
, is a smooth family of diffeomorphisms
[0,1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}[0,1]
such that
A_{h}(0)=0
,
A_{h}(1)=1
, and
A_{0}=\operatorname{id}
.

Given a function
f\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
, its
T
-twisted difference quotients
are:

(A.2)

D^{T_{h}}f=\frac{f(x+T_{h}(x))-f(x)}{h}.

We now prove the analog of Lemma
A.1
, following the proof of
[
Wen08
, Theorem A.1.22]
.

Lemma A.4
.

Assume
1\leq p<\infty
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

(1)

If
f\in W^{k,p}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{n})
, then
D^{T_{h}}f
converges to
(\partial_{h}T_{h})|_{h=0}\cdot f^{\prime}

in
W^{k-1,p}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{n})
as
h\to 0
.

(2)

Suppose
p>1
,
f\in W^{k-1,p}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and
\|D^{T_{h}}f\|_{W^{k-1,p}}\leq C
for some
C>0
and all
h>0
in a neighborhood of
0\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then:

(a)

f\in W^{k,p}(\tilde{I},\mathbb{R}^{n})
for any closed segment
\tilde{I}\subset[0,1]
that does not intersect
\{0,1\}
.

(b)

If the
\|D^{T_{h}}f\|_{W^{k-1,p}}
are uniformly bounded over all choices of
T_{h}
satisfying (
A.1
), then
f\in W^{k,p}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Sketch of proof.

(1) Given
f\in C^{1}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we claim that the sequence
D^{T_{h}}f
converges uniformly to
(\partial_{h}T_{h})|_{h=0}\cdot f^{\prime}
. We compute:

\displaystyle|D^{T_{h}}f(x)-(\partial_{h}T_{h})|_{h=0}(x)f^{\prime}(x)|=|f^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\tfrac{T_{h}(x)}{h}-(\partial_{h}T_{h})|_{h=0}(x)f^{\prime}(x))|

\displaystyle\leq|\tfrac{T_{h}(x)}{h}(f^{\prime}(x^{\prime})-f^{\prime}(x))|+|f^{\prime}(x)(\tfrac{T_{h}(x)}{h}-(\partial_{h}T_{h})|_{h=0}(x))|,

where
x^{\prime}
is chosen in the
T_{h}(x)
-neighborhood of
x
to satisfy the mean value theorem. Applying the uniform continuity of
f^{\prime}(x)
and the uniform convergence of
\tfrac{T_{h}(x)}{h}
to
(\partial_{h}T_{h})|_{h=0}
implies the claim.

Now let
f\in W^{k,p}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{n})
. We show that for
h>0
small there exists
C>0
such that

(A.3)

\left\|D^{T_{h}}f\right\|_{L^{p}}\leq C\left\|\tfrac{T_{h}(x)}{h}f^{\prime}\right\|_{L^{p}},

by observing the following sequence of inequalities

(A.4)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}|D^{T_{h}}f|^{p}dx=\int_{0}^{1}\left|\tfrac{T_{h}(x)}{h}\int_{0}^{1}f^{\prime}(x+\tau T_{h}(x))d\tau\right|^{p}dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\tfrac{T_{h}(x)}{h}f^{\prime}(x+\tau T_{h}(x))\right|^{p}dxd\tau\leq C^{p}\int_{0}^{1}\left\|\tfrac{T_{h}(x)}{h}f^{\prime}\right\|^{p}_{L^{p}}d\tau=C^{p}\left\|\tfrac{T_{h}(x)}{h}f^{\prime}\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}.

Finally we claim that, given
\varepsilon>0
, for
h
sufficiently small we have:

(A.5)

\left\|D^{T_{h}}f-(\partial_{h}T_{h})|_{h=0}f^{\prime}\right\|_{L^{p}}<\varepsilon.

Indeed, pick a smooth function
f_{\varepsilon}
which is very close to
f
in the
L^{p}
-norm and observe that

(A.6)

\left\|D^{T_{h}}f-D^{T_{h}}f_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{p}}<\left\|\tfrac{T_{h}(x)}{h}(f^{\prime}-f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})\right\|_{L^{p}}

by (
A.3
). Then (
A.5
) follows from (
A.6
) and the convergence for
f_{\varepsilon}
shown earlier; see
[
Wen08
]
for more details. The case
k>1
follows by applying (
A.5
) to the derivatives of
f
.

(2)(a) Let
\widetilde{T}_{h}
be a family of functions on
[0,1]
satisfying

(\operatorname{id}+\widetilde{T}_{-h})(\operatorname{id}+T_{h})=\operatorname{id}.

This family also satisfies (
A.1
).

We will treat the case
k=1
; the case
k>1
follows by applying the same proof to the derivatives of
f
. Suppose
f\in L^{p}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\|D^{T_{h}}f\|_{L^{p}}\leq C
for some
C>0
and all
h>0
in a neighborhood of
0\in\mathbb{R}
.
Observe that
D^{T_{h}}f\cdot\frac{h}{T_{h}(x)}
is bounded in the
L^{p}
-norm over
\tilde{I}
as
(\partial_{h}T_{h})|_{h=0}\geq\kappa>0
on
\tilde{I}
. Hence, by the Banach-Alaoglu theorem, there is a sequence
h_{j}\to 0
such that the corresponding sequence of functions
D^{T_{h_{j}}}f\cdot\frac{h_{j}}{T_{h_{j}}(x)}
converges in the
L^{p}
-norm to a function
g
.

We claim that
g
is a weak derivative of
f
along
\tilde{I}
. Indeed, for any
\varphi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\tilde{I},\mathbb{R}^{n})
and small
h>0
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\tilde{I}}g\varphi dx=

\displaystyle\lim_{h_{j}\to 0}\int_{\tilde{I}}\tfrac{f(x+T_{h_{j}}(x))-f(x)}{T_{h_{j}}(x)}\varphi dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{h_{j}\to 0}\int_{\tilde{I}}\tfrac{f(u)}{-\widetilde{T}_{-h_{j}}(u)}\varphi(u+\widetilde{T}_{-h_{j}}(u))\cdot(1+\partial_{u}\widetilde{T}_{-h_{j}}(u))du-\lim_{h_{j}\to 0}\int_{\tilde{I}}\tfrac{f(u)}{T_{h_{j}}(u)}\varphi(u)du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{h_{j}\to 0}\int_{\tilde{I}}f(u)\tfrac{\varphi(u+\widetilde{T}_{-h_{j}}(u))-\varphi(u)}{-\widetilde{T}_{-h_{j}}(u)}du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\tilde{I}}f(u)\varphi^{\prime}(u)du.

Hence
g
is a weak derivative of
f
along
\tilde{I}
, which implies that
f|_{\tilde{I}}\in W^{1,p}(\tilde{I},\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

(2)(b) We treat the
k=1
case. Under our assumption that
\|D^{T_{h}}f\|_{L^{p}}
are uniformly bounded over all choices of
T_{h}
satisfying (
A.1
), it suffices to find a family of the form
T_{h}(x)=h\tilde{t}(x)
with
\tilde{t}(x)
smooth, such that
D^{T_{h}}f\cdot\frac{h}{T_{h}(x)}=\tfrac{f(x+T_{h}(x))-f(x)}{T_{h}(x)}
have bounded
L^{p}
-norms on
[0,1]
. We can take such a function
\tilde{t}(x)
to be a smoothing of a staircase function
t(x)
of the following form:
given a decreasing sequence
b_{n}\to 0
there is a choice of a sequence
a_{n}\to 0
with
a_{n}<1/2
, such that

t(x)=b_{n}
for
x\in[a_{n+1},a_{n}]
and
x\in[1-a_{n},1-a_{n+1}]
. Details on how to use such a
T_{h}(x)
are left to the reader.
∎

Corollary A.5
.

The following statements are equivalent for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
:

(1)

The
L^{2}
-map
\gamma\colon[0,1]\to M
is an element of
W^{k,2}([0,1],M)
.

(2)

For any smooth family of functions
T\coloneqq(T_{h})_{h\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})}
satisfying (
A.1
), the path

\displaystyle P_{T}(\gamma)\colon(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\to W^{k-1,2}([0,1],M),\quad h\mapsto\gamma\circ A_{h},

is differentiable at
h=0
with respect to the smooth structure on
W^{k-1,2}([0,1],M)
.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Lemma
A.4
.
∎

A.2.
Regularity for unstable manifolds.

In Appendix
A.2
we show that the unstable manifolds of a certain smooth pseudogradient vector field of an action functional
\mathscr{A}_{L}
on the space

\Omega^{1,2}(M^{n};q,q^{\prime})=\{\gamma\in W^{1,2}([0,1],M^{n})\mid\gamma(0)=q,\,\gamma(1)=q^{\prime}\}

consist of smooth paths.

Lemma A.6
.

For each smooth family
T\coloneqq(T_{h})_{h\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})}
of functions satisfying (
A.1
), let

N_{T}
be a finite-dimensional manifold and
\iota_{T}:N_{T}\to(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})_{h}\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
a smooth immersion
such that:

(1)

the set
\Xi\coloneqq\iota_{T}(N_{T})\cap\{h=0\}
is independent of
T
; and

(2)

for each
(0,\gamma)\in\Xi
and
T
the path

\widetilde{P}_{T}(\gamma):(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\to(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime}),\quad h\mapsto(h,\gamma\circ A_{h}),

is a subset of
\iota_{T}(N_{T})
.

Then the elements of
\Xi
are smooth paths.

Proof.

Consider the natural inclusion
\phi_{k}\colon W^{k,2}([0,1],M)\to W^{k-1,2}([0,1],M)
,
f\mapsto f
.

We treat the
k=1
case; the same argument works inductively for
k>1
. The map
(\operatorname{id}\times\phi_{1})\circ\iota_{T}
is a smooth immersion for each
T
since
N_{T}
is finite-dimensional and hence the inclusion of the tangent space at each point is a closed embedding.

Given
(0,\gamma)\in\Xi
and
T
, the path
(\operatorname{id}\times\phi_{1})\circ\widetilde{P}_{T}(\gamma)
(i.e., the path
\widetilde{P}_{T}(\gamma)
viewed as a path in
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{0,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
) is differentiable at
h=0
by Corollary
A.5
. Since this path has image in
(\operatorname{id}\times\phi_{1})\circ\iota_{T}(N_{T})
, it is differentiable with respect to the smooth structure on this immersed submanifold and hence is differentiable as a path in
N_{T}
. Therefore,
\widetilde{P}_{T}(\gamma)
is a differentiable map to
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
and
\gamma\in\Omega^{2,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
by applying Corollary
A.5
again.
∎

We now recall the setup from
[
AS09
]
in more detail. Consider a
time-dependent coordinate system,
i.e., a smooth map

\varphi\colon[0,1]\times U\to M,

where
U
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
containing
0
,
\varphi|_{\{t\}\times U}
is a diffeomorphism onto the open subset
\varphi(\{t\}\times U)
for all
t\in[0,1]
, and
\varphi(0,0)=q,\varphi(1,0)=q^{\prime}
. Then there is an associated chart of
\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})

\displaystyle\varphi_{*}\colon\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)\to\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\gamma\mapsto\varphi(\cdot,\gamma(\cdot))).

Here
\varphi(\cdot,\gamma(\cdot))
is shorthand for a map
t\mapsto\varphi(t,\gamma(t)).

Following the notation in
[
AS09
]
, we require such charts
(U,\varphi)
to be
bi-bounded
, meaning that
U
is bounded and the image
\varphi([0,1]\times U)
has compact closure, and all derivatives of
\varphi
and of
(t,q)\mapsto\varphi(t,\cdot)^{-1}(q)
are bounded. We denote the image of
\varphi_{*}
by
\mathscr{U}_{(U,\varphi)}
; it is said to be
centered at the path
\varphi(t,0)
. Such charts form a smooth atlas of
\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
.

Example A.7
.

The standard example of a time-dependent coordinate system is given as follows: Let
\gamma\in\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
be a smooth path. The pullback bundle
\gamma^{*}TM
admits a trivialization
\psi\colon[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\gamma^{*}TM
and let
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a small open ball centered at
0
. Then we define
\varphi:[0,1]\times U\to M
via

\varphi(t,\xi)=\exp_{\gamma(t)}(\gamma_{*}\psi(\xi)),

where the exponential map is with respect to some metric
g
.

Similarly, for
(a,b)\subset(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})
, we consider a smooth map

\tilde{\varphi}\colon(a,b)\times[0,1]\times U\to M,

where
U
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
containing
0
,
\tilde{\varphi}|_{\{h\}\times\{t\}\times U}
is a diffeomorphism onto the open subset
\tilde{\varphi}(\{h\}\times\{t\}\times U)
for all
(h,t)\in(a,b)\times[0,1]
, and
\tilde{\varphi}(h,0,0)=q,\tilde{\varphi}(h,1,0)=q^{\prime}
for all
h\in(a,b)
. There is a similar notion of bi-boundedness for such a pair
(U,\tilde{\varphi})
. To such a pair we associate a smooth chart
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}_{(U,\tilde{\varphi})}
of
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
via

(A.7)

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}_{*}\colon(a,b)\times\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)\to(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime}),

\displaystyle(h,\gamma)\mapsto(h,\tilde{\varphi}(h,\cdot,\gamma(\cdot))).

The charts
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}_{(U,\tilde{\varphi})}
form a smooth atlas of
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
.

Example A.8
.

For
(U,\varphi)
bi-bounded and
A=(A_{h}=\operatorname{id}+T_{h})_{h\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})}
, we define the map

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}^{A}\colon(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times[0,1]\times U\to M.

\displaystyle(h,t,x)\mapsto\varphi(A_{h}(t),x),

The map
\tilde{\varphi}^{A}
is smooth and the pair
(U,\tilde{\varphi}^{A})
is bi-bounded. The associated chart
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}_{(U,\tilde{\varphi}^{A})}
of
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
then has the form

(A.8)

\tilde{\varphi}^{A}_{*}(h,\gamma)=(h,\varphi(A_{h}(\cdot),\gamma(\cdot))).

The family
A
induces a family of smooth maps

(A.9)

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{h}:\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})\to(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\gamma\mapsto(h,\gamma\circ A_{h}).

Similarly there is a (local) family of smooth maps

\widetilde{A}_{h}\colon\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)\to(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(U,0,0),

\gamma\mapsto(h,\gamma\circ A_{h}),

with the same notation by abuse of notation.

We claim that the following commutativity relation is satisfied for any
h\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})
.

(A.10)

\tilde{\varphi}^{A}_{*}\circ\widetilde{A}_{h}=\widetilde{A}_{h}\circ\varphi_{*},

as it is straightforward to check:

\tilde{\varphi}^{A}_{*}(h,\gamma\circ A_{h}(\cdot)))=(h,\varphi(A_{h}(\cdot),\gamma\circ A_{h}(\cdot))))=(h,\varphi_{*}(\gamma)\circ A_{h}(\cdot)).

Remark A.9
.

The equivariance (
A.10
) allows us to work with the
\widetilde{A}_{h}
-action directly in the chart
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}_{(U,\tilde{\varphi}^{A})}
.

Next we define several related Lagrangian functions. Given a Lagrangian
L\colon[0,1]\times TM\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying the assumptions of
[
AS09
, Section 3]
and a family
A=(A_{h})
, we define

(A.11)

\displaystyle L_{h}:[0,1]\times TM\to\mathbb{R},\quad(t,x,v)\mapsto\partial_{t}A_{h}(t)\cdot L\left(A_{h}(t),x,\tfrac{v}{\partial_{t}A_{h}(t)}\right),

\displaystyle L_{A}\colon(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times[0,1]\times TM\to\mathbb{R},\quad(h,t,x,v)\mapsto L_{h}(t,x,v).

Given charts
(U,\varphi)
and
(U,\tilde{\varphi}^{A})
we define related pullbacks:

\displaystyle\varphi^{*}L\colon[0,1]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R},\quad(t,x,v)\mapsto L(t,\varphi(t,x),D_{x}\varphi(t,x)(v)),

\displaystyle(\varphi^{*}L)_{h}\colon[0,1]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R},\quad(t,x,v)\mapsto\partial_{t}A_{h}(t)\cdot\varphi^{*}L\left(A_{h}(t),x,\tfrac{v}{\partial_{t}A(t)}\right),

\displaystyle(\tilde{\varphi}^{A})^{*}L_{A}\colon(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times[0,1]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R},\quad(h,t,x,v)\mapsto L_{A}(h,t,\tilde{\varphi}^{A}(h,t,x),D_{x}\tilde{\varphi}^{A}(h,t,x)(v)).

One verifies that

(A.12)

(\varphi^{*}L)_{h}=(\tilde{\varphi}^{A})^{*}(L_{h}).

This equation allows us to work with the family
L_{h}
locally in the chart associated with
(U,\tilde{\varphi}^{A})
and from now on we will allow ourselves to write
L
instead of
\varphi^{*}(L)
and
L_{h}
instead of
(\tilde{\varphi}^{A})^{*}(L_{h})
when a specific chart is understood.

We are now ready to prove the main theorem of Appendix
A
. The proof follows the general outline of
[
AS09
, Theorem 4.1]
with an extra ingredient: the parameter
h\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})
and a corresponding family of diffeomorphisms
(A_{h}:[0,1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}[0,1])
.

Theorem A.10
.

There exists a choice of smooth pseudogradient vector field
X
for a given action functional
\mathscr{A}_{L}
on
\Omega^{1,2}(M,q_{0},q_{1})
such that all unstable manifolds consist of smooth paths.

Proof.

Let
T=(T_{h})_{h\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})}
be a smooth family of functions satisfying (
A.1
) and let

A=(A_{h}=\operatorname{id}+T_{h})

be the associated family of diffeomorphisms. If
\gamma_{\operatorname{crit}}
is a critical point of
\mathscr{A}_{L}
, then
P_{h}(\gamma_{\operatorname{crit}})\coloneqq\gamma_{\operatorname{crit}}\circ A_{h}
is a critical point of
\mathscr{A}_{L_{h}}
. By
[
AS09
, Proposition 3.1]
,
\gamma_{\operatorname{crit}}
is smooth, and hence the path

\widetilde{P}_{T}(\gamma_{\operatorname{crit}})=\{(h,P_{h}(\gamma_{\operatorname{crit}}))\mid h\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\}

is a smooth
1
-dimensional submanifold of
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
.

The goal is to construct a smooth vector field
\widetilde{X}
on
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
which satisfies the following conditions:

(a)

The component of
\widetilde{X}
in the
\partial_{h}
-direction is zero;

(b)

X\coloneqq\widetilde{X}|_{\{0\}\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})}
is a pseudogradient vector field of
\mathscr{A}_{L}
which is independent of
T
;

(c)

\widetilde{X}=0
along all the paths
\widetilde{P}_{T}(\gamma_{\operatorname{crit}})
;

(d)

\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{A}_{h}(0,\gamma)}=d\widetilde{A}_{h}(\widetilde{X}_{(0,\gamma)})
for any
\gamma\in W^{u}(\gamma_{\operatorname{crit}};X)
, where
\widetilde{A}_{h}
is given by Equation (
A.9
).

We note that (b) and (d) imply

(d’)

the unstable manifolds
W^{u}(\widetilde{P}_{T}(\gamma_{\operatorname{crit}});\widetilde{X})
satisfy the conditions of Lemma
A.6
.

Indeed, for any
h\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})
,
\widetilde{A}_{h}
takes the trajectory of
\widetilde{X}
from
(0,\gamma_{\operatorname{crit}})
to
(0,\gamma)
to the trajectory of
\widetilde{X}
from
(h,P_{h}(\gamma_{\operatorname{crit}}))
to
(h,P_{h}(\gamma))
, giving Lemma
A.6
(2). Moreover Lemma
A.6
(1) holds because
X
is independent of
T
.
The existence of such a vector field
\widetilde{X}
for each
T
and Lemma
A.6
imply the theorem.

Let
\gamma_{0}\in\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
be a smooth path. We consider a time-dependent coordinate system
\varphi\colon[0,1]\times U\to M
such that
\varphi(t,0)=\gamma_{0}(t)
and the associated charts
\mathscr{U}_{(U,\varphi)}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}_{(U,\tilde{\varphi}^{A})}
centered at
\gamma_{0}
.

To construct
\widetilde{X}
we construct
\widetilde{Y}
on the charts
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}_{(U,\tilde{\varphi}^{A})}
centered at
\gamma_{0}
and apply a partition of unity argument.

\gamma_{0}
non-critical.
Suppose
\gamma_{0}
is a non-critical point of
\mathscr{A}_{L}
. Let
Y_{0}
be a tangent vector at
0\in\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)
such that
d\varphi(Y_{0})
is a smooth vector field along
\gamma_{0}
which is sufficiently close to the gradient vector field
\operatorname{grad}_{\mathscr{A}_{L}}(\gamma_{0})
with respect to the
W^{1,2}
-norm (note that since we take the gradient with respect to the
W^{1,2}
-metric it is not immediate that such gradient is smooth as in the case of the
L^{2}
-metric). We extend
Y_{0}
to
Y
on all of
\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)
by setting
Y_{\gamma}=Y_{0}
for all
\gamma\in\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)
. Next, we extend
Y
to
\widetilde{Y}
on
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)
by

(A.13)

\widetilde{Y}_{(h,\gamma)}(\cdot)=d\widetilde{A}_{h}(Y_{0})_{\widetilde{A}_{h}(0)}(\cdot)=Y_{0}(A_{h}(\cdot)).

The term
Y_{0}(A_{h}(\cdot))
does not depend on
\gamma
but depends on
h
. Since
Y_{0}
is a smooth vector field along
\gamma_{0}
,
\widetilde{Y}
is smooth in the
h
-direction and hence smooth on
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)
. Furthermore, by definition
\widetilde{Y}
satisfies the invariance condition

(A.14)

\widetilde{Y}_{\widetilde{A}_{h}(0,\gamma)}=d\widetilde{A}_{h}(\widetilde{Y}_{(0,\gamma})),

for any
\gamma\in\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)
. Hence, by (
A.10
),
d\tilde{\varphi}^{A}(\widetilde{Y})
satisfies (d) over
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}_{(U,\tilde{\varphi}^{A})}
.

\gamma_{0}
critical.
Let
\gamma_{0}
be a critical point of
\mathscr{A}_{L}
. Note that
\gamma_{0}
is a smooth path. Recall from
[
AS09
, Equations (3.8),(3.10)]
that the Gateaux differential
d\mathscr{A}_{L}
and the second Gateaux differential
d^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L}
at
\gamma\in\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)
have expressions

(A.15)

d\mathscr{A}_{L}(\gamma)[\xi]=\int_{0}^{1}(\partial_{x}L(t,\gamma,\dot{\gamma})\xi+\partial_{v}L(t,\gamma,\dot{\gamma})\dot{\xi})dt,

(A.16)

\displaystyle d^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L}(\gamma)[\xi,\eta]=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}L}{\partial v^{2}}(t,\gamma,\dot{\gamma})\dot{\xi}\dot{\eta}+\frac{\partial^{2}L}{\partial v\partial x}(t,\gamma,\dot{\gamma})\dot{\xi}\eta+\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\partial^{2}L}{\partial x\partial v}(t,\gamma,\dot{\gamma})\xi\dot{\eta}+\frac{\partial^{2}L}{\partial x^{2}}(t,\gamma,\dot{\gamma})\xi\eta\right)dt,

for any
\xi,\eta\in\Omega^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n};0,0)\subset W^{1,2}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{n})
. There is also an associated linear operator

\displaystyle D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L}(\gamma)\colon\Omega^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n};0,0)\to\Omega^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n};0,0)^{*},

\displaystyle D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L}(\gamma)(\xi)[\eta]=d^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L}(\gamma)[\xi,\eta].

The pseudogradient
Y
constructed in the proof of
[
AS09
, Theorem 4.1]
satisfies

\langle Y_{\gamma},\eta\rangle_{0}=D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L}(0)(\gamma)[\eta],\quad\gamma\in\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0),

where the Hilbert inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{0}
is given by

\langle\xi,\eta\rangle_{0}=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}L}{\partial v^{2}}(t,0,0)\dot{\xi}\cdot\dot{\eta}+\xi\cdot\eta\right)dt

on
W^{1,2}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{n})
. We remark that
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{0}
is equivalent to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{{W^{1,2}}}
. To extend
Y
to
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)
let us define a family of Hilbert products on
\Omega^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n};0,0)
varying over
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})
by the formula

\langle\xi,\eta\rangle_{h}=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}L_{h}}{\partial v^{2}}(t,0,0)\dot{\xi}\cdot\dot{\eta}+(\partial_{t}A_{h})\xi\cdot\eta\right)dt.

This family of Hilbert products satisfies

(A.17)

\widetilde{A}_{h}^{*}\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{h}=\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{0},

which is verified by a direct computation

\displaystyle\langle d\widetilde{A}_{h}(\xi),d\widetilde{A}_{h}(\eta)\rangle_{h}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{1}{\partial_{t}A_{h}}\frac{\partial^{2}L}{\partial v^{2}}(A_{h}(t),0,0)(\partial_{t}A_{h})^{2}\dot{\xi}(A_{h}(t))\cdot\dot{\eta}(A_{h}(t))+(\partial_{t}A_{h})\xi(A_{h}(t))\cdot\eta(A_{h}(t)\right)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}L}{\partial v^{2}}(t,0,0)\xi(t)\cdot\eta(t)+\xi(t)\cdot\eta(t)\right)dt=\langle\xi,\eta\rangle_{0},

using the change-of-variables formula. The product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{h}
varies smoothly with respect to
h
since
L_{h}
and its partial derivatives vary smoothly.

Let
\widetilde{Y}
be the vector field satisfying

(A.18)

\langle\widetilde{Y}_{(h,\gamma)},\eta\rangle_{h}=D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L_{h}}(0)(\gamma)[\eta],

for any
(h,\gamma)\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)
and
\eta\in\Omega^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n};0,0)
.
Clearly
\widetilde{Y}
is a smooth vector field since
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{h}
and
D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L_{h}}
vary smoothly with respect to
h
and
D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L_{h}}(0)
is linear for each value of
h\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})
.

We claim that

(A.19)

D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L_{h}}(0)(P_{h}(\gamma))[d\widetilde{A}_{h}(\eta)]=D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L}(0)(\gamma)[\eta]

for each
\gamma\in\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)
, which is again verified by the direct computation

\displaystyle D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L_{h}}(0)

\displaystyle(P_{h}(\gamma))[d\widetilde{A}_{h}(\eta)]=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\partial_{t}A_{h}\frac{\partial^{2}L}{\partial v^{2}}(A_{h}(t),0,0)\dot{\gamma}(A_{h}(t))\cdot\dot{\eta}(A_{h}(t))\right.

\displaystyle+\partial_{t}A_{h}\frac{\partial^{2}L}{\partial x\partial v}(A_{h}(t),0,0)\gamma(A_{h}(t))\cdot\dot{\eta}(A_{h}(t))+\partial_{t}A_{h}\frac{\partial^{2}L}{\partial v\partial x}(A_{h}(t),0,0)\dot{\gamma}(A_{h}(t))\cdot\eta(A_{h}(t))

\displaystyle\left.+\partial_{t}A_{h}\frac{\partial^{2}L}{\partial x^{2}}(A_{h}(t),0,0)\gamma(A_{h}(t))\cdot\eta(A_{h}(t))\right)dt=D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L}(0)(\gamma)[\eta],

using the change-of-variables formula.

Combining (
A.17
) and (
A.19
), for any
\gamma\in\Omega^{1,2}(U;0,0)
and
h\in(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})
,

\displaystyle\langle d\widetilde{A}_{h}(\widetilde{Y}_{(0,\gamma)}),d\widetilde{A}_{h}(\eta)\rangle_{h}=D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L}(0)(\gamma)[\eta]=D^{2}\mathscr{A}_{L_{h}}(0)(P_{h}(\gamma))[d\widetilde{A}_{h}(\eta)]=\langle\widetilde{Y}_{\widetilde{A}_{h}(0,\gamma)},d\widetilde{A}_{h}(\eta)\rangle_{h}.

Hence the invariance condition (
A.14
) holds for
\widetilde{Y}
.

Partition of unity argument.

Pick a cover of
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
by open charts of the form
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}_{(U,\tilde{\varphi}^{A})}
. Denote by
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}_{\gamma}
such an open chart centered at the smooth curve
\gamma
and
\mathscr{U}_{\gamma}
the corresponding chart of the form
\mathscr{U}_{(U,\varphi)}
centered at
\gamma
. By paracompactness, there is a locally finite, countable subcover
\{\mathscr{U}_{\gamma_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
of the cover

\{\mathscr{U}_{\gamma}\mid\gamma\in C^{\infty}([0,1],M)\cap\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})\}.

Writing
\varphi_{j}
for the smooth map associated with the chart
\mathscr{U}_{\gamma_{j}}
, let
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}_{\gamma_{j}}
be the image of
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times(\varphi_{j})_{*}^{-1}(\mathscr{U}_{\gamma_{j}})
under
(\tilde{\varphi}_{j})_{*}^{A}
.

Let
\{\psi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
be a partition of unity subordinate to the cover
\{\mathscr{U}_{\gamma_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
. We define a smooth function
\widetilde{\psi}_{j}
supported on
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}_{\gamma_{j}}
by setting

\widetilde{\psi}_{j}(h,\gamma(\cdot))=\psi_{j}(\gamma(A_{h}^{-1}(\cdot))

for each point
(h,\gamma)
in
(-\varepsilon_{T},\varepsilon_{T})\times(\varphi_{j})_{*}^{-1}(\mathscr{U}_{\gamma_{j}})
. Notice that
\{\widetilde{\psi}_{j}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
constitute a partition of unity and each one of them satisfies
\widetilde{\psi}_{j}(\widetilde{A}_{h}(0,\gamma))=\widetilde{\psi}_{j}(0,\gamma)
.
Finally, we set

(A.20)

\widetilde{X}=\textstyle\sum_{j\in\mathscr{J}}\widetilde{\psi}_{j}\widetilde{Y}_{\mathscr{I}(j)},

where
\widetilde{Y}_{\mathscr{I}(j)}
is of one of two types constructed above depending on whether
{\mathscr{I}(j)}
is a critical point of
\mathscr{A}_{L}
. Combining the invariance properties of
\widetilde{\psi}_{j}
and of
\widetilde{Y}_{\mathscr{I}(j)}
shown above we conclude that
\widetilde{X}
indeed satisfies (d). Since our construction is compatible with the one in
[
AS09
, Theorem 4.1]
, the restriction of
\widetilde{X}
to
\{0\}\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,q,q^{\prime})
is guaranteed to be a pseudogradient.
∎

Appendix B
Transversality

B.1.
Morse flow lines with switchings.

As in the proof of Theorem
A.10
(now we are in the case of arbitrary
\kappa\geq 1
), there exists a countable locally finite cover
\{\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
of
\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
, where
\bm{\gamma}_{j}=(\gamma_{j1},\dots,\gamma_{j\kappa})
is a
\kappa
-tuple of smooth paths and
\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{j}}
is the product of the corresponding
\mathscr{U}_{\gamma_{ji}}
. We also assume the following:

(C1)

if some
\gamma\in\mathscr{U}_{\gamma_{ji}}
is a critical point, then
\gamma
lies in only one chart
\mathscr{U}_{\gamma_{ji}}
(called a
critical point chart
) and in that case
\gamma=\gamma_{ji}
;

(C2)

critical point charts will be arbitrarily small and will be shrunk as necessary in response to given submanifolds in
[0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}).

Let
\mathfrak{X}_{j}
be the subspace of
T_{\bm{\gamma}_{j}}\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
which is equal to the direct sum

V_{j1}\oplus\dots\oplus V_{j\kappa}\subset T_{\gamma_{j1}}\Omega^{1,2}(M,q_{1},q^{\prime}_{\sigma(1)})\oplus\dots\oplus T_{\gamma_{j\kappa}}\Omega^{1,2}(M,q_{\kappa},q^{\prime}_{\sigma(\kappa)})

where
V_{ji}=C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}(\bm{\gamma}_{ji}^{*}TM)
if
\gamma_{ji}
is not a critical point and
0
otherwise. Here we denote by
C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}(\bm{\gamma}_{ji}^{*}TM)
the separable Banach space of sections
Y
with finite Floer
C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}
-norm
[
Flo88
]
:

\|Y\|_{C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}}=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\varepsilon_{k}\|Y\|_{C^{k}}<\infty,

where
\bm{\varepsilon}=(\varepsilon_{k})_{k\geq 1}
is a fixed sequence of sufficiently small positive numbers. We pick the sequence
\bm{\varepsilon}
such that for each section
Y^{\prime}\in W^{1,2}(\bm{\gamma}_{ji}^{*}TM)
and each point
p
on
\gamma_{ji}
there is a section
Y\in C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}(\bm{\gamma}_{ji}^{*}TM)
satisfying
Y(p)=Y^{\prime}(p)
and
\|Y-Y^{\prime}\|_{W^{1,2}}
is smaller than a given
\delta>0
. The existence of such
\bm{\varepsilon}
is shown in
[
Wen08
, Theorem B.6]
. Moreover we fix a choice of
\bm{\varepsilon}
that satisfies the above property for all
\bm{\gamma}_{j}
with
j\in\mathscr{J}
.

We regard an element
Y_{j}\in\mathfrak{X}_{j}
as a vector field over
\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{j}}
.
Consider the direct sum
\bigoplus_{j\in\mathscr{J}}\mathfrak{X}_{j}
with supremum norm, i.e., it consists of bounded sequences
\{Y_{j}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
. Clearly, it is a separable Banach space. We fix a partition of unity
\{\psi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
subordinate to the cover
\{\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
. We set

(B.1)

\mathfrak{X}=\left\{X\in\Gamma(T\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}))\mid\,X=\sum_{j\in\mathscr{J}}\psi_{j}Y_{j},\,\text{ for some }(Y_{j})_{j}\in\bigoplus_{j\in\mathscr{J}}\mathfrak{X}_{j}\right\}.

For a fine enough cover
\{\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
this space, which is a Banach subspace of the Banach space of bounded sections of
T\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
, is isomorphic to the direct sum
\bigoplus_{j\in\mathscr{J}}\mathfrak{X}_{j}
. In particular, it is a separable smooth Banach manifold.

Lemma B.1
.

Given
\bm{Z}=(Z_{1},\dots,Z_{\kappa})\in T_{\bm{\gamma}}\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
for a smooth tuple of paths
\bm{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{\kappa})\in\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
such that
\bm{Z}
is of class
C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}
along
\bm{\gamma}
and
Z_{k}=0
whenever
\gamma_{k}
is in a critical point chart, there exists a vector field
X\in\mathfrak{X}
such that
X_{\bm{\gamma}}=Z
.

Proof.

Let
\{\psi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
be a partition of unity subordinate to the cover
\{\mathscr{U}_{\gamma_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
of
\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
.
To construct the vector field
X\in\mathfrak{X}
, we set
Y_{i}=Z/\psi_{i}(\bm{\gamma})
for some
i
such that
\bm{\gamma}\in\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{i}}
and
\psi_{i}(\bm{\gamma})\not=0
, and set
Y_{j}=0
on
\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{j}}
for
j\neq i
. Then
(Y_{j})_{j}\in\bigoplus_{j\in\mathscr{J}}\mathfrak{X}_{j}
and
X=\sum\psi_{j}Y_{j}
is the desired vector field.
∎

Since
\mathfrak{X}
is separable, the space of maps
C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}([0,1],\mathfrak{X})
is a separable Banach space. The “parameter” spaces that we use are subspaces of spaces of a similar type and hence are also separable, allowing us to use the Sard-Smale theorem. In particular, we set

(B.2)

\mathfrak{X}_{-}=\{X\in C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}((-\infty,0],\mathfrak{X})\,\mid\,X(s)=0\,\,\forall s\leq-1\ \},

(B.3)

\mathfrak{X}_{+}=\{X\in C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}([0,+\infty),\mathfrak{X})\,\mid\,X(s)=0\,\,\forall s\geq 1\ \}

Next, fix a nondecreasing smooth function
g\colon[0,+\infty)\to[0,+\infty)
such that
g(l)=\frac{l}{3}
for
l\leq 1
and
g(l)=1
for
l\geq 3
, and define

(B.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{0}=\{X\in C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}([0,+\infty)^{2},\mathfrak{X})\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ \mbox{$X(l,s)=0$ for $s\in(g(l),\operatorname{max}(g(l),l-1))\cup[l,+\infty)$};

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to+\infty}\operatorname{split}X(l,\cdot)\text{ exists in }\mathfrak{X}_{+}\times\mathfrak{X}_{-};\text{ all partial derivatives}

\displaystyle\text{ of }X\text{ vanish at }(0,s)\text{ for all }s\in[0,+\infty)\},

where, writing
X_{l}(s)\coloneqq X(l,s)
and
X_{l}^{T}(s)\coloneqq X_{l}(s+T)
,

\operatorname{split}X_{l}=(X_{l}|_{[0,1]}\#0|_{[1,+\infty)},0|_{(-\infty,-1]}\#X_{l}^{l+1}|_{[-1,0]}).

We refer to
[
Mes18
, Remark 5.8]
for an explanation of the technical condition on the vanishing of the partial derivatives in the definition. For our purposes we will only need the restriction
X_{l}|_{[0,l]}
and quite often we will regard
X_{l}
as a path of vector fields
X_{l}\in C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}([0,l],\mathfrak{X})
.

Fix a pseudogradient vector field
X
(i.e., satisfying (PG1)–(PG5)) for a given Lagrangian
L\colon[0,1]\times TM\to\mathbb{R}
on
\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
satisfying Theorem
A.10
.

Given a critical point
\bm{\gamma}
, we set

(B.5)

\mathscr{P}_{-}(\bm{\gamma})=\{\Gamma\in C^{1}((-\infty,0],\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})\mid\,\lim_{s\to-\infty}\Gamma=\bm{\gamma},\,\lim_{s\to-\infty}\partial_{s}\Gamma=0\},

which is a smooth Banach manifold. There is a Banach vector bundle
\mathscr{F}_{-}
over
\mathscr{P}_{-}(\bm{\gamma})
with fibers

\mathscr{F}_{-}|_{\Gamma}=C^{0}_{0}(\Gamma^{*}(T\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}))),

where the subscript
0
means that the limit
s\to-\infty
is equal to
0
. Then the unstable manifold of
\bm{\gamma}
with respect to
X
can be identified with the set of zeros of the section

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{-}(\bm{\gamma})\to\mathscr{F}_{-},\quad\Gamma\mapsto(\partial_{s}\Gamma+X)|_{\Gamma(s)},

via the evaluation map
\Gamma\mapsto\Gamma(0)
.

More generally, there is a vector bundle
\mathscr{F}_{-}
over
\mathscr{P}_{-}(\bm{\gamma})\times\mathfrak{X}_{-}

with fibers

\mathscr{F}_{-}|_{(\Gamma,Y)}=C^{0}_{0}(\Gamma^{*}(T\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}))),

and a section

\displaystyle\sigma_{-}\colon\mathscr{P}_{-}(\bm{\gamma})\times\mathfrak{X}_{-}\to\mathscr{F}_{-},\quad(\Gamma,Y)\mapsto(\partial_{s}\Gamma+X+Y)|_{\Gamma(s)}.

We then define

(B.6)

W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})\coloneqq\sigma_{-}^{-1}(0),\quad W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};Y)=W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})\cap(\mathscr{P}_{-}(\bm{\gamma})\times\{Y\}).

We also have an evaluation map

(B.7)

E_{-}\colon W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})\to\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}),\quad(\Gamma,Y)\mapsto\Gamma(0).

Lemma B.2
.

(1)

The space
W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})
is smooth Banach manifold.

(2)

The evaluation map
E_{-}
has image in the space of smooth paths
C^{\infty}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
.

(3)

Let
U
be an open subset of
W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})
consisting of
(\Gamma,Y)
such that
\Gamma(0)
is contained in a neighborhood
\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{j}}=\mathscr{U}_{\gamma_{j1}}\times\dots\times\mathscr{U}_{\gamma_{j\kappa}}
with all
\gamma_{ji}
non-critical.
Then the differential of
E_{-}
restricted to
U
has any
C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}
-section in its image.

Proof.

(1) As in
[
Sch93
, Theorem 12]
one can show that
\sigma_{-}
is a smooth map. Hence it remains to show that
\sigma_{-}
is transverse to the
0
-section. We compute:

D\sigma_{-}(\Gamma,Y)(\Xi,Z)=(\nabla_{s}\Xi+\nabla_{\Xi}(X+Y)+Z)|_{\Gamma(s)}.

Its surjectivity is standard and shown in
[
AM06
, Proposition 1.6]
(in fact the
Z
term is not necessary for the surjectivity). Hence
W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})
is a smooth Banach manifold.

(2) One can show as in Appendix
A.2
that for each
Y\in\mathfrak{X}_{-}
the unstable manifold
W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};Y)
consists of smooth paths. Hence the image of
E_{-}
is contained in
C^{\infty}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
.

(3) Let
(\Gamma,Y)\in U
. Given
v\in T_{\Gamma(0)}\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
with finite
C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}
-norm, we claim that there exists

(\Xi,Z)\in\operatorname{ker}D\sigma_{-}(\Gamma,Y)=T_{(\Gamma,Y)}W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})

such that
DE_{-}(\Gamma,Y)(\Xi,Z)=v.
To this end, let
\Xi=0
(and hence satisfies

\nabla_{s}\Xi(s)+\nabla_{\Xi}(X+Y)=0
) on
s\in(-\infty,-1]

and extend
\Xi
over
s\in[-1,0]
so that
\Xi(0)=v
,
\Xi\in T_{\Gamma}\mathscr{P}_{-}(\bm{\gamma})
, and

Z(s)|_{\Gamma(s)}=-\nabla_{s}\Xi(s)-\nabla_{\Xi(s)}(X_{\Gamma(s)}+Y(s)|_{\Gamma(s)})

is nonzero only near
s=0
where
\Gamma(s)\in\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{j}}
. Moreover we can guarantee that it has finite
C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}
-norm as a section over
(-\infty,0]
.

Finally, using the method of proof of Lemma
B.1
, there exists an extension of
Z(s)|_{\Gamma(s)}
,
s\in(-\infty,0]
, to a vector field
Z(s)\in\mathfrak{X}
, subject to
Z
being a
C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}
-section over
(-\infty,0]
.

Explicitly, let
\{\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{j_{1}}},\dots,\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{j_{k}}}\}
be a finite subcollection of
\{\mathscr{U}_{\bm{\gamma}_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathscr{J}}
covering the image of
[-1,0]
under
\Gamma
and let
\nu_{j_{1}},\dots,\nu_{j_{k}}\colon[-1,0]\to\mathbb{R}
be a partition of unity subordinate to the pullback cover of
[-1,0]
. We then set

X_{i}(s)=\frac{Z(s)|_{\Gamma(s)}\nu_{i}(s)}{\psi_{i}},\quad\text{ for $i=j_{1},\dots,j_{k}$ \penalty\ \penalty\ and \penalty\ \penalty\ $\psi_{i}\not=0$},

and set
X_{i}=0
otherwise. Then
Z(s)=\sum\psi_{i}X_{i}(s)
is the desired family of vector fields in
\mathfrak{X}_{-}
.
∎

Lemma B.3
.

Let
N\subset[0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
be a
C^{m-1}
-submanifold of codimension
n>0
which is disjoint from

(B.8)

\tilde{\mathscr{C}}\coloneqq[0,1]^{\ell}\times\{\bm{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{\kappa})\mid\mbox{some $\gamma_{i}$ is a critical point }\},

and, at each point
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\gamma})\in N
with
\bm{\gamma}
a tuple of smooth paths,

(B.9)

\displaystyle\text{the tangent space }T_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\gamma})}N\text{ is transverse to the space of $C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}$-sections along $\bm{\gamma}$, viewed as a subset of}

\displaystyle\{0\}\times T\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})\subset\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\times T\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}).

Then:

(1)

The space

\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},N;\mathfrak{X}_{-})\coloneqq\{(\bm{\theta},\Gamma)\in[0,1]^{\ell}\times W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})\mid(\bm{\theta},\Gamma(0))\in N\}

is a
C^{m-1}
-smooth Banach manifold.
3
3

3

“Thickened” versions of
W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})
etc. obtained by multiplying by
[0,1]^{\ell}
will have tildes over them.

(2)

For a generic
Y\in\mathfrak{X}_{-}
, provided
m>\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})-n
,

\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},N;Y)\coloneqq\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},N;\mathfrak{X}_{-})\cap W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};Y)

is a
C^{m-1}
-smooth manifold of dimension
\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})+\ell-n
and the evaluation map

\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},N;Y)\to N,\quad(\bm{\theta},\Gamma)\mapsto\Gamma(0),

is an immersion.

Proof.

(1) Consider the evaluation map (a “thickening” of
E_{-}
)

(B.10)

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{-}\colon[0,1]^{\ell}\times W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})\to[0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle(\bm{\theta},\Gamma)\mapsto(\bm{\theta},\Gamma(0)).

There exists an open subset
U\subset W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})
such that
N\cap\operatorname{Im}(\tilde{E}_{-})\subset\tilde{E}_{-}([0,1]^{\ell}\times U)
and
U
satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
B.2
(3). This is because
N
is a submanifold and (C2) allows us to shrink critical point charts in response to
N
. Hence differential of
\tilde{E}_{-}
on
U
by Lemma
B.2
(3) has any
C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}
-section in its image and using (
B.9
) we conclude that the preimage
\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},N;\mathfrak{X}_{-})=\tilde{E}_{-}^{-1}(N)
is a
C^{m-1}
-submanifold.

(2) Let
\tilde{\sigma}_{-}=\operatorname{id}_{[0,1]^{\ell}}\times\sigma_{-}
and let
D_{1}\tilde{\sigma}_{-}{(\bm{\theta},\Gamma,Y)}
be the component of
D\tilde{\sigma}_{-}{(\bm{\theta},\Gamma,Y)}
in the
(\bm{\theta},\Gamma)
-direction, i.e.,

\displaystyle D_{1}\tilde{\sigma}_{-}{(\bm{\theta},\Gamma,Y)}\colon\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\times C^{1}_{0}(\Gamma^{*}T\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}))\to C^{0}_{0}(\Gamma^{*}T\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})),

\displaystyle(\varphi,\Xi)\mapsto\nabla_{s}\Xi+\nabla_{\Xi}(X+Y);

it is Fredholm of index
\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})+\ell
at any point
(\bm{\theta},\Gamma,Y)\in[0,1]^{\ell}\times W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})
.
Here we have used the fact that
X|_{\Gamma}
is (at least)
C^{m}
. Then
\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},N;\mathfrak{X}_{-})=(\tilde{\sigma}_{-}|_{\mathscr{P}_{-}(\bm{\gamma},N)\times\mathfrak{X}_{-}})^{-1}(0)
, where

\mathscr{P}_{-}(\bm{\gamma},N)=\{(\bm{\theta},\Gamma)\in[0,1]^{\ell}\times\mathscr{P}_{-}(\bm{\gamma})\mid(\bm{\theta},\Gamma(0))\in N\}.

The tangent space of this manifold at
(\bm{\theta},\Gamma)
is given by those
(\varphi,\Xi)
such that
(\varphi,\Xi(0))\in TN
, which is a codimension
n
linear subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\times C^{1}_{0}(\Gamma^{*}T\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}))
. The restriction of
D_{1}\tilde{\sigma}_{-}
to this subspace has index
\operatorname{ind}(D_{1}\tilde{\sigma}_{-})-n
and
the claim now follows from
[
MS12
, Lemma A.3.6]
and the Sard-Smale theorem.
∎

Remark B.4
.

The manifold
\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},N;\mathfrak{X}_{-})
is a fiber product

([0,1]^{\ell}\times W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-}))\times_{[0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})}N

and fits into the following pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},N;\mathfrak{X}_{-})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[0,1]^{\ell}\times W^{u}(\bm{\gamma};\mathfrak{X}_{-})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{E}_{-}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{E}_{-}}

Let
N_{1},N_{2}
be
C^{m-1}
-smooth manifolds and let
\iota_{j}\colon N_{j}\to[0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
be
C^{m-1}
-smooth maps for
j=1,2
. We introduce the following spaces (note that we often write
N_{j}
instead of
(N_{j},\iota_{j})
, for simplicity):

(B.11)

\displaystyle\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},N_{2};\mathfrak{X}_{0})=\{(\bm{\theta},l,\Gamma,Y,x_{1},x_{2})\mid\bm{\theta}\in[0,1]^{\ell},\,l>0,

\displaystyle\Gamma\colon[0,l]\to[0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}),Y\in\mathfrak{X}_{0},(x_{1},x_{2})\in N_{1}\times N_{2},

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\Gamma+X+Y=0,(\bm{\theta},\Gamma(0))=\iota_{1}(x_{1}),(\bm{\theta},\Gamma(l))=\iota_{2}(x_{2})\},

(B.12)

\displaystyle\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})=\{(\bm{\theta},l,\Gamma,Y,x_{1})\mid\bm{\theta}\in[0,1]^{\ell},\,l>0,

\displaystyle\Gamma\colon[0,l]\to\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}),\,Y\in\mathfrak{X}_{0},x_{1}\in N_{1},

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\Gamma+X+Y=0,(\bm{\theta},\Gamma(0))=\iota_{1}(x_{1})\},

(B.13)

\displaystyle\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,N_{2};\mathfrak{X}_{0})=\{(\bm{\theta},l,\Gamma,Y,x_{2})\mid\bm{\theta}\in[0,1]^{\ell},\,l>0,

\displaystyle\Gamma\colon[0,l]\to\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}),\,Y\in\mathfrak{X}_{0},x_{2}\in N_{2},

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\Gamma+X+Y=0,(\bm{\theta},\Gamma(l))=\iota_{2}(x_{2})\},

(B.14)

\displaystyle\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})=\{(\bm{\theta},l,\Gamma,Y)\mid\bm{\theta}\in[0,1]^{\ell},\,l>0,

\displaystyle\Gamma\colon[0,l]\to\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}),\,Y\in\mathfrak{X}_{0},\,\partial_{s}\Gamma+X+Y=0\}.

The superscript “
ft
” stands for “finite time”.

The space
\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})
comes with an evaluation map

(B.15)

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{0}=(\tilde{E}_{0}^{s},\tilde{E}_{0}^{t})\colon\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})\to([0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}))^{2},

\displaystyle(\bm{\theta},l,\Gamma,Y)\mapsto(\bm{\theta},\Gamma(0),\bm{\theta},\Gamma(l)).

The evaluation maps of the other spaces are defined analogously.

Lemma B.5
.

Let
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
be
C^{m-1}
-smooth Banach manifolds and
\iota_{j}\colon N_{j}\to[0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
,
j=1,2
, be
C^{m-1}
-immersions such that
\operatorname{dim}N_{1}
is finite and
\iota(N_{1})\subset[0,1]^{\ell}\times C^{\infty}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
,
\iota_{2}
is Fredholm,
\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
intersect transversely, and

(*)

at each point
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\gamma})
of
\iota_{2}(N_{2})
which belongs to
[0,1]^{\ell}\times C^{\infty}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
,
T_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\gamma})}\iota_{2}(N_{2})
is transverse to the space of
C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}
-sections as in (
B.9
); and

(**)

\iota_{2}(N_{2})
is disjoint from an open neighborhood of
\tilde{\mathscr{C}}
given by (
B.8
).

Then:

(1)

\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})
is a
C^{m}
-smooth Banach manifold with boundary.

(2)

\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},N_{2};\mathfrak{X}_{0})
and
\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})
are
C^{m-1}
-smooth Banach manifolds with boundary.

(3)

For a generic
Y\in\mathfrak{X}_{0}
,
\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},N_{2};Y)
and
\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},-;Y)
are smooth manifolds with boundary and

(B.16)

\displaystyle\operatorname{dim}\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},N_{2};Y)=\operatorname{dim}N_{1}+1-\operatorname{codim}\iota_{2}(N_{2}),

(B.17)

\displaystyle\operatorname{dim}\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},-;Y)=\operatorname{dim}N_{1}+1.

Sketch of proof.

(1) There is a
C^{m}
-diffeomorphism

\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})\cong[0,1]^{\ell}\times[0,+\infty)\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})\times\mathfrak{X}_{0},

which associates to any
(\bm{\theta},l,\bm{\gamma},Y)
a unique trajectory of length
l
starting at
\gamma
along
Y
.

(2) This is analogous to the proof of Lemma
B.3
(1) and a detailed account in the finite-dimensional setting can be found in
[
Mes18
, Theorem 1.21]
.

We briefly discuss the case
\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},N_{2};\mathfrak{X}_{0})
; the case of
\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})
is straightforward.
As in the proof of Lemma
B.3
(1), Condition (**) and (C2) allow us to shrink critical point charts in response to
\iota_{2}(N_{2})
. By the proof of Lemma
B.2
(3) and Condition (*), the map
\tilde{E}_{0}
is transverse to
(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})
. (Roughly speaking, the transversality with
\iota_{1}
is freely achieved, and the transversality with
\iota_{2}
is a constrained one, analogous to the construction of
(\Xi,Z)
which are zero away from
s=0
in the proof of Lemma
B.2
(3).)
(2) then follows since

\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},N_{2};\mathfrak{X}_{0})=N_{1}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\iota_{1}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-12.1664pt{\iota_{1}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\iota_{1}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-12.1664pt{\iota_{1}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\iota_{1}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-9.23198pt{\iota_{1}}\kern 5.6321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\iota_{1}}}}}\times^{{\kern-4.15433pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-8.1431pt{\iota_{1}}\kern 4.54321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.98769pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\iota_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.98769pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\iota_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-13.54878pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 5.6321pt{\iota_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-4.15433pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-12.4599pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 4.54321pt{\iota_{2}}}}}N_{2},

which is a
C^{m-1}
-smooth Banach manifold.

(3) We will treat the
\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},N_{2};Y)
case. Consider the manifold

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{(\bm{\theta},l,\Gamma,x_{1},x_{2})\mid\bm{\theta}\in[0,1]^{\ell},l\in[0;+\infty),\Gamma\colon[0,l]\to\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}),(x_{1},x_{2})\in N_{1}\times N_{2},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad(\bm{\theta},\Gamma(0))=\iota_{1}(x_{1}),(\bm{\theta},\Gamma(l))=\iota_{2}(x_{2})\}.

Then
\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},N_{2};\mathfrak{X}_{0})
is the zero set of the following map:

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}_{0}\colon\mathscr{P}(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}\to\mathscr{F}_{0},\quad(\bm{\theta},l,\Gamma,x_{1},x_{2},Y)\mapsto\partial_{s}\Gamma+X+Y_{l},

where
\mathscr{F}_{0}
is the vector bundle over
\mathscr{P}(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}
with fiber

\mathscr{F}_{0}|_{(\bm{\theta},l,\Gamma,x_{1},x_{2},Y)}=C^{0}_{0}(\Gamma^{*}(T\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}))).

Here the unstable manifold
\tilde{W}^{ft}(N_{1},-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})
consists of (trajectories along) smooth paths, by our choice of
X
and construction of
\mathfrak{X}_{0}
, and an argument similar to that of the proof of Theorem
A.10
.

As before, we write
D_{1}\sigma_{0}
for the component of
D\sigma_{0}
that does not depend on the variation along the parameter space
\mathfrak{X}_{0}
. Then by the existence and uniqueness of solutions of ODEs we conclude that
\operatorname{dim}\operatorname{ker}D_{1}\sigma_{0}=\operatorname{dim}N_{1}+1
, and by
[
AM06
, Lemma 2.21 (iii)]
this operator is surjective. Clearly, the restriction of
D_{1}\sigma_{0}
to
\mathscr{P}(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}
then has index
\operatorname{dim}N_{1}+1-\operatorname{codim}_{\iota_{2}}N_{2}
as in the proof of Lemma
B.3
, which concludes the stated index computation.
∎

We are finally in a position to introduce the cast of characters that appear in the statement and the proof of the main result (Theorem
B.6
) of Appendix
B.1
.

Let
\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime}\in\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
be critical points,
\ell
a positive integer, and
\bm{\tau}=(\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{\ell})
an
\ell
-tuple of pairs
\tau_{1}=\{i_{1},j_{1}\},\dots,\tau_{\ell}=\{i_{\ell},j_{\ell}\}
of distinct elements of
\{1,\dots,\kappa\}
. Recall from Section
2.2
that we have switching maps
sw_{k}^{ij}\colon\overline{\Delta}_{I_{k}}^{ij}\to\overline{\Delta}_{I_{k}}^{ij}
.
Let
\bm{s}=(s_{1},\dots,s_{\ell})
be a total order on
[\ell]
and let

\bm{Y}=(Y_{-},Y_{1},\dots,Y_{\ell-1},Y_{+})\in\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}.

Then we set:

(B.18)

\displaystyle\tilde{W}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\Delta_{I_{s_{\ell}}}^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}};\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},{\Delta}_{I_{s_{1}}}^{i_{1}j_{1}};\mathfrak{X}_{-})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-31.6385pt{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-31.6385pt{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-26.8034pt{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 5.6321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-4.15433pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-25.71452pt{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 4.54321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,\Delta^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{t_{s_{2}}};\mathfrak{X}_{0})

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{I_{s_{2}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-32.22185pt{sw^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{I_{s_{2}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{I_{s_{2}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-32.22185pt{sw^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{I_{s_{2}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{I_{s_{2}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-27.38675pt{sw^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{I_{s_{2}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 5.6321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{I_{s_{2}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-4.15433pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-26.29787pt{sw^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{I_{s_{2}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 4.54321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}\dots\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{\ell-1}j_{\ell-1}}_{I_{s_{\ell-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-37.47185pt{sw^{i_{\ell-1}j_{\ell-1}}_{I_{s_{\ell-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{\ell-1}j_{\ell-1}}_{I_{s_{\ell-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-37.47185pt{sw^{i_{\ell-1}j_{\ell-1}}_{I_{s_{\ell-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{\ell-1}j_{\ell-1}}_{I_{s_{\ell-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-32.63675pt{sw^{i_{\ell-1}j_{\ell-1}}_{I_{s_{\ell-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 5.6321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{\ell-1}j_{\ell-1}}_{I_{s_{\ell-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-4.15433pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-31.54787pt{sw^{i_{\ell-1}j_{\ell-1}}_{I_{s_{\ell-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 4.54321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,\Delta_{I_{s_{\ell}}}^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}};\mathfrak{X}_{0}),

(B.19)

\displaystyle\tilde{W}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},\Delta_{I_{s_{1}}}^{i_{1}j_{1}};\mathfrak{X}_{-})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-31.6385pt{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-31.6385pt{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-26.8034pt{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 5.6321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-4.15433pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-25.71452pt{sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 4.54321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,\Delta^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{t_{s_{2}}};\mathfrak{X}_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{I_{s_{2}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-32.22185pt{sw^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{I_{s_{2}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{2}j_{2}}_{I_{s_{2}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-31.54787pt{sw^{i_{\ell-1}j_{\ell-1}}_{I_{s_{\ell-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 4.54321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,\Delta_{I_{\ell}}^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}};\mathfrak{X}_{0})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-31.63853pt{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{+}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-31.63853pt{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{+}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern
