4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-25.71455pt{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 4.54321pt{\tilde{E}_{+}}}}}\tilde{W}^{s}(\Delta_{I_{s_{\ell}}}^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\mathfrak{X}_{+}).

The fiber
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
over
\bm{Y}
of the natural projection

\tilde{W}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})\to\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}

is the space of Morse flow lines with switchings (corresponding to the permutation
\bm{s}
) from Definition
2.8
. For
i=0,\dots,\ell-1
there exists an evaluation map

(B.20)

\tilde{E}_{i}\colon\tilde{W}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})\to\operatorname{Conf}_{\ell}([0,1])\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}),

given by an intermediate evaluation map of the form
\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}
in (
B.19
). Its restriction to
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
, also denoted
\tilde{E}_{i}
, sends
\bm{\Gamma}
to
(\bm{\theta},\Gamma_{i}(l_{i}))
, using the notation from Definition
2.8
.

We also introduce some notation to describe higher-codimensional phenomena in the moduli spaces
\tilde{W}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
, i.e., when many switches occur at the same moment.

Let
k_{1}+\dots+k_{r}=\ell
be a partition of
\ell
(with all
k_{i}
positive). Write
\bm{\tau}=(\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}},\dots,\bm{\tau}_{k_{r}})
, where
\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}=(\{i_{1},j_{1}\},\dots,\{i_{k_{1}},j_{k_{1}}\})
consists of the first
k_{1}
elements of
\bm{\tau}
,
\bm{\tau}_{k_{2}}
consists of the next
k_{2}
elements of
\bm{\tau}
, etc. We have closed subsets

(B.21)

\Delta^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}=\Delta^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}_{I_{s_{1}},\dots,I_{s_{k_{1}}}},\dots,\Delta^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{r}}}=\Delta^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{r}}}_{I_{s_{\ell-k_{r}+1}},\dots,I_{s_{\ell}}}\subset\operatorname{Conf}_{\ell}([0,1])\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime}),

as defined in (
2.10
), whose restrictions to
\operatorname{Conf}_{\ell}([0,1])\times\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
are
C^{m-1}
submanifolds (and we denote these restrictions similarly by abuse of notation).

As discussed at the end of Section
2.2
, the closure
\overline{\Delta}^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{j}}}
in
[0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
can be stratified by
C^{m-k_{j}}
submanifolds of codimension at least
k_{j}n
for each
j\colon 1\leq j\leq r
.

We use the notation

(B.22)

sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}=sw^{i_{k_{1}}j_{k_{1}}}_{I_{s_{k_{1}}}}\circ\dots\circ sw^{i_{1}j_{1}}_{I_{s_{1}}}\colon\overline{\Delta}^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}\to\overline{\Delta}^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}},

etc. for the composition of switching maps on the locus where it is well-defined. Let
\bm{N}=(N_{1},\dots,N_{r})
, where
N_{j}
is one of the strata of
\overline{\Delta}^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{j}}}
from the stratification that we mentioned above.
We define

(B.23)

\displaystyle\tilde{W}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\bm{N};\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=

\displaystyle\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},N_{1};\mathfrak{X}_{-})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-30.50336pt{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-30.50336pt{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-25.66826pt{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 5.6321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}}}}\times^{{\kern-4.15433pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-24.57938pt{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{-}}\kern 4.54321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}\dots\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{r}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-31.01297pt{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{r}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{E_{+}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{r}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-31.01297pt{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{r}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{E_{+}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{r}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-26.17787pt{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{r}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 5.6321pt{E_{+}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{r}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-4.15433pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-25.08899pt{sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{r}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 4.54321pt{E_{+}}}}}\tilde{W}^{s}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},N_{r};\mathfrak{X}_{+}),

by analogy with Equation (
B.19
). By the discussion right after Definition
2.4
, the restrictions of
sw^{\bm{\tau}_{k_{j}}}
to the strata
N_{j}
are
C^{m-k_{j}}
-smooth. We note that there are only
r-1
spaces of finite-length trajectories in the above definition, although in the notation we declare that we use
\ell-1
corresponding perturbation spaces. That is because for each
k_{j}
we “collapse”
k_{j}-1
finite-length trajectories and the corresponding perturbations are not used.
The fiber over
\bm{Y}\in\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}
is denoted by

(B.24)

\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\bm{N};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s}).

We also write:

\displaystyle\tilde{W}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\Delta_{I_{s_{i}},I_{s_{i+1}}}^{\tau_{i},\tau_{i+1}};\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\tilde{W}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\bm{N};\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+},\bm{\tau},\bm{s}),

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\Delta_{I_{s_{i}},I_{s_{i+1}}}^{\tau_{i},\tau_{i+1}};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\bm{N};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s}),

where
(k_{1},\dots,k_{i-1},k_{i},k_{i+1},\dots,k_{\ell-1})=(1,\dots,1,2,1,\dots,1)
,
N_{i}=\Delta_{I_{s_{i}},I_{s_{i+1}}}^{\tau_{i},\tau_{i+1}}
, and
N_{j}=\Delta^{\tau_{j}}_{I_{j}}
for
j\not=i
.

The following is the main result of Appendix
B.1
:

Theorem B.6
.

(1)

For a generic
\bm{Y}\in\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}
, given any
\bm{\gamma}\in\mathscr{P}_{\sigma}
,
\bm{\gamma}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
,
\ell
, and
\bm{\tau}
, the space
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
is a smooth manifold with corners of dimension

(B.25)

\operatorname{dim}\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})-\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime})-(n-2)\ell-1,

(2)

For a generic
\bm{Y}
, given any collection of submanifolds
\bm{N}=(N_{1},\dots,N_{r})
associated with a given tuple
\bm{\tau}
and a partition
(k_{1},\dots,k_{r})
of
\ell
, the space
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\bm{N};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
is a smooth manifold of dimension at most

(B.26)

\operatorname{dim}\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\bm{N};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})-\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime})-(n-1)\ell+r-1,

where
k=\operatorname{max}(k_{1},\dots,k_{r})
.

Proof.

(1) We fix some
m\gg 1
and inductively prove that
\tilde{W}_{k}\coloneqq\tilde{W}_{k}(\bm{\gamma},\Delta_{I_{s_{k}}}^{i_{k}j_{k}};\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{k-1},\bm{\tau}|_{k},\bm{s}|_{k})
is a
C^{m-1}
-smooth Banach manifold, where
1\leq k\leq\ell
and
\bm{\tau}|_{k}=(\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{k})
.

Lemma
B.3
provides the base case
k=1
of the induction, i.e., for

\tilde{W}_{1}=\tilde{W}^{u}(\bm{\gamma},\Delta_{I_{s_{1}}}^{i_{1}j_{1}};\mathfrak{X}_{-}).
It suffices to verify Condition (
B.9
), which is indeed satisfied since for any
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\gamma})\in\Delta_{I_{s_{1}}}^{i_{1}j_{1}}
, there exists a
C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}
-section along
\gamma_{i_{1}}
which points at
\theta_{s_{1}}
in an arbitrary given direction
X\in T_{\gamma_{i_{1}}(\theta_{s_{1}})}M
, by our choice of
\bm{\varepsilon}
. Such sections clearly form a subspace transverse to the tangent space
T_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\gamma})}\Delta_{I_{s_{1}}}^{i_{1}j_{1}}
.

For
k>1
we have:

\displaystyle\tilde{W}_{k}=\tilde{W}_{k-1}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{k-1}j_{k-1}}_{I_{s_{k-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-38.41972pt{sw^{i_{k-1}j_{k-1}}_{I_{s_{k-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{k-1}j_{k-1}}_{I_{s_{k-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-38.41972pt{sw^{i_{k-1}j_{k-1}}_{I_{s_{k-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{k-1}j_{k-1}}_{I_{s_{k-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-33.58463pt{sw^{i_{k-1}j_{k-1}}_{I_{s_{k-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 5.6321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{k-1}j_{k-1}}_{I_{s_{k-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-4.15433pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-32.49574pt{sw^{i_{k-1}j_{k-1}}_{I_{s_{k-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 4.54321pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}}}}}\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.98769pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.98769pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-13.54878pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-4.15433pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-12.4599pt{\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Delta^{i_{k}j_{k}}_{I_{s_{k}}}.

As in the
k=1
case,
\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}\colon\tilde{W}^{ft}(-,-;\mathfrak{X}_{0})\to[0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{1,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
is transverse to
\Delta^{i_{k}j_{k}}_{I_{s_{k}}}
at any point
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\gamma})\in\Delta^{i_{k}j_{k}}_{I_{s_{k}}}
with
\bm{\gamma}\in C^{\infty}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
. The transversality of
sw^{i_{k-1}j_{k-1}}_{I_{s_{k-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}
and
\tilde{E}_{0}^{s}
is achieved freely as in the proof of Lemma
B.5
, and
\tilde{W}_{k}
is a
C^{m-1}
-smooth Banach manifold. Note that
\tilde{W}_{k}
consists of trajectories of smooth paths by Appendix
A.2
. Hence
\tilde{W}_{\ell}
is a
C^{m-1}
-smooth Banach manifold.

By a similar argument,

\tilde{W}_{\ell}^{\dagger}\coloneqq\tilde{W}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\tilde{W}_{\ell}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-31.63853pt{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{+}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-7.68211pt\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-31.63853pt{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 8.071pt{\tilde{E}_{+}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-5.24321pt\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-26.80344pt{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 5.6321pt{\tilde{E}_{+}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}}}}\times^{{\kern-4.15433pt\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-25.71455pt{sw^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}}_{I_{s_{\ell}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}}\kern 4.54321pt{\tilde{E}_{+}}}}}\tilde{W}^{s}(\Delta_{I_{s_{\ell}}}^{i_{\ell}j_{\ell}},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\mathfrak{X}_{+})

is a
C^{m-1}
-smooth Banach manifold.

Finally, by the arguments from Lemmas
B.3
and
B.5
, the projection
\tilde{W}_{\ell}^{\dagger}\to\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}
is Fredholm of index

\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})-\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime})-(n-2)\ell-1
. Here we rely on the fact that
sw^{i_{k-1}j_{k-1}}_{I_{s_{k-1}}}\circ\tilde{E}_{0}^{t}
is an immersion for any
k
when restricted to a portion of
\tilde{W}_{k}
corresponding to any given choice of perturbation data.
Then it follows from the Sard-Smale theorem that for a generic
\bm{Y}
the space
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
is a
C^{m-1}
manifold of dimension stated in (1). Since
m
in the above argument is arbitrary we conclude that this space is a smooth manifold.

(2) The above proof can be repeated by replacing
\Delta^{i_{k}j_{k}}_{I_{s_{k}}}
by the appropriate
N_{k}
. We point out again that our definition of switching maps guarantees that the restrictions to the strata
N_{k}
regarded as subsets of
[0,1]^{\ell}\times\Omega^{m,2}(M,\bm{q},\bm{q}^{\prime})
are at least
C^{m-k}
-smooth.
∎

B.2.

.

has index

The only additional step compared to that of Theorem
B.6
(1)(2) is that the differential of concatenation
c^{\bm{w}}
is an injective linear map at any point for any choice of weights
\bm{w}
. (1) then follows from the Sard-Smale theorem.

Appendix C
Compactness

C.1.
Construction of a consistent collection of perturbation data

The goal of this subsection is to construct a consistent collection of perturbation spaces so that the boundaries of
\mathscr{M}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
and
\mathscr{M}_{T}(\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\bm{\gamma}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\bm{\gamma}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\bm{\gamma}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\bm{\gamma}\hfil$\crcr}}},\bm{\gamma}_{0};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
are covered by products of moduli spaces for smaller
\ell
and subtrees of
T
. The perturbation spaces
\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}
from Appendix
B
are clearly not sufficient since our choices of
\bm{Y}\in\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times(\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{\ell-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}
for various
\ell
,
\bm{\tau}
, and
\bm{s}
are unrelated. The classical approach from
[
Sei08
]
requires making a choice of consistent perturbation data
\bm{Y}
for all
\ell
and all trees
T
. Our construction will follow the approach of Abouzaid
[
Abo11
, Lemma 7.2]
and Mescher
[
Mes18
, Section 2]
for the finite-dimensional case of Morse gradient trees.

The construction of a consistent collection of perturbation spaces is rather involved and will be carried out over the next few pages.

The domains of our trajectories
\bm{\Gamma}
are parametrized by
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}:=[0,+\infty)^{\ell}
,
\ell=0,1,2,\dots
, where
\ell+1
is the number of switches and if
(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{\ell})\in\mathscr{D}_{\ell}
, then
\lambda_{i}
is the length of the trajectory between the
(i-1)
st switch and the
i
th switch.

Our basic building blocks are the spaces
\mathfrak{X}_{\pm}(k)
and
\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{i}(k)
,
k=0,1,2,\dots
,
i=1,\dots,k
. (They are components of
\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell)
below.) We set
\mathfrak{X}_{\pm}(0)=\mathfrak{X}_{\pm}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{0}(0)=\mathfrak{X}_{0}
and for
k>0
we recursively define

(C.1)

\mathfrak{X}_{-}(k)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}Y\in C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}(\mathscr{D}_{k}\times(-\infty,0],\mathfrak{X})\,\,\mid\,Y(\lambda,\cdot)\in\mathfrak{X}_{-}\penalty\ \penalty\ \forall\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{k})\in\mathscr{D}_{k},\\
\lim_{\lambda_{j}^{\prime}\rightarrow+\infty}Y|_{\lambda_{j}=\lambda_{j}^{\prime}}\text{ exists in }\mathfrak{X}_{-}(k-1)\penalty\ \penalty\ \forall j\in\{1,2,\dots,k\}\end{array}\right\};

(C.2)

\mathfrak{X}_{+}(k)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}Y\in C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}(\mathscr{D}_{k}\times[0,+\infty),\mathfrak{X})\,\,\mid\,Y(\lambda,\cdot)\in\mathfrak{X}_{+}\penalty\ \penalty\ \forall\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{k})\in\mathscr{D}_{k},\\
\lim_{\lambda_{j}^{\prime}\rightarrow+\infty}Y|_{\lambda_{j}=\lambda_{j}^{\prime}}\text{ exists in }\mathfrak{X}_{+}(k-1)\penalty\ \penalty\ \forall j\in\{1,2,\dots,k\}\end{array}\right\};

(C.3)

\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{i}(k)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}Y\in C_{\bm{\varepsilon}}(\mathscr{D}_{k}\times[0,+\infty),\mathfrak{X})\mid Y_{\lambda,i}\in\mathfrak{X}_{0}\penalty\ \penalty\ \forall\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{k})\in\mathscr{D}_{k},\\
\lim_{\lambda_{j}^{\prime}\rightarrow+\infty}Y|_{\lambda_{j}=\lambda_{j}^{\prime}}\text{ exists in $\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{i-1}(k-1)$ or in $\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{i}(k-1)$}\\
\penalty\ \penalty\ \forall j\in\{1,\dots,\hat{i},\dots,k\}\text{\penalty\ depending on whether $j<i$ or $j>i$},\\
\lim_{\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\to+\infty}\operatorname{split}Y|_{\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{i}^{\prime}}\text{ exists in }\mathfrak{X}_{+}(k-1)\times\mathfrak{X}_{-}(k-1)\end{array}\right\},

where
\hat{\cdot}
means the term is omitted, and
Y_{\lambda,i}\in\mathfrak{X}_{0}
is
Y(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{i-1},\cdot,\lambda_{i+1},\dots,\lambda_{k},\cdot)
, i.e., with all but the
i
th and last spots specified.

Next we define the
background perturbation space
for
\mathfrak{X}_{\pm}(k)
for
k\geq 1
:

(C.4)

\mathfrak{X}_{\pm}^{\operatorname{back}}(k)\coloneqq\left\{\bm{Z}=(Z_{1},\dots,Z_{k})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\pm}(k-1)^{\times k}\mid\lim_{\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}Z_{j}|_{\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{i}^{\prime}}=\lim_{\lambda_{j-1}^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}Z_{i}|_{\lambda_{j-1}=\lambda_{j-1}^{\prime}}\penalty\ \forall j>i\right\}.

Viewing
\lambda_{j}=+\infty
as the faces of
\mathscr{D}_{k}
at
+\infty
, there is a “face map” which assigns to
Y\in\mathfrak{X}_{\pm}(k)
its
background perturbation data
4
4

4

We keep Mescher’s terminology of “background perturbation data”, but perhaps it’s better to call it “boundary perturbation data”, where the boundary refers to the boundary at
+\infty
.

(C.5)

\displaystyle F_{\pm,k}:\mathfrak{X}_{\pm}(k)\to\mathfrak{X}_{\pm}^{\operatorname{back}}(k),\quad Y\mapsto(\lim_{\lambda_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}Y|_{\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{1}^{\prime}},\dots,\lim_{\lambda_{k}^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}Y|_{\lambda_{k}=\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}).

Similarly, we define the background perturbation spaces

(C.6)

\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{\operatorname{back},i}(k)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{Z}^{\prime}=(Z_{1},\dots,\widehat{Z}_{i},\dots Z_{k},Z^{+},Z^{-})\in\\
\mathfrak{X}^{i-1}_{0}(k-1)^{\times i-1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{i}(k-1)^{\times k-i}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}(k-1)\times\mathfrak{X}_{-}(k-1)\bigm|\\
\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda_{a}^{\prime}\rightarrow+\infty}Z_{b}|_{\lambda_{a}=\lambda_{a}^{\prime}}=\lim_{\lambda_{b-1}^{\prime}\rightarrow+\infty}Z_{a}|_{\lambda_{b-1}=\lambda_{b-1}^{\prime}}\quad\forall a<b\in\{1,\dots,\hat{i},\dots,k\},\\
\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\to+\infty}\operatorname{split}Z_{a}|_{\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{i}^{\prime}}=\lim_{\lambda_{a-1}^{\prime}\rightarrow+\infty}(Z^{+}|_{\lambda_{a-1}=\lambda_{a-1}^{\prime}},Z^{-}|_{\lambda_{a-1}=\lambda_{a-1}^{\prime}})\quad\forall a>i,\\
\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda_{i-1}^{\prime}\to+\infty}\operatorname{split}Z_{a}|_{\lambda_{i-1}=\lambda_{i-1}^{\prime}}=\lim_{\lambda_{a}^{\prime}\rightarrow+\infty}(Z^{+}|_{\lambda_{a}=\lambda_{a}^{\prime}},Z^{-}|_{\lambda_{a}=\lambda_{a}^{\prime}})\quad\forall a<i\end{array}\right\},

and there is a “face map”

(C.7)

\displaystyle F_{0,k,i}:\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{i}(k)\to\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{\operatorname{back},i}(k),

\displaystyle Y\mapsto(\lim_{\lambda_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}Y|_{\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{1}^{\prime}},\dots,\widehat{\lim_{\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}Y|_{\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{i}^{\prime}}},\dots,\lim_{\lambda_{k}^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}Y|_{\lambda_{k}=\lambda_{k}^{\prime}},\lim_{\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\to\infty}\operatorname{split}Y|_{\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{i}^{\prime}}).

Given
\bm{Z}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{\pm}(k)
and
\bm{Z}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back},i}_{0}(k)
we set
\mathfrak{X}_{\pm}(k,\bm{Z}):=F_{\pm,k}^{-1}(\bm{Z})
and
\mathfrak{X}_{0}(k,\bm{Z}^{\prime}):=F_{0,k,i}^{-1}(\bm{Z}^{\prime})
.

We now assemble the above building blocks into spaces
\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell)
,
\ell\geq 1
, of
\ell
-switching perturbation data
and
\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell)
of
background
\ell
-switching perturbation data
: Given

\bm{\tau}=(\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{\ell})
and an ordering
\bm{s}
on
[\ell]
, we set:

(C.8)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\mathfrak{X}_{-}(\ell-1)\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{1}(\ell-1)\times\dots\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{\ell-1}(\ell-1)\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}(\ell-1),

(C.9)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell)=\prod_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}),

(C.10)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{-}(\ell-1)\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{\operatorname{back},1}(\ell-1)\times\dots\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{\operatorname{back},\ell-1}(\ell-1)\times\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{+}(\ell-1),

(C.11)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell)=\prod_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}).

When
\ell=1
there is no background perturbation data. We remark that the right-hand sides of (
C.8
) and (
C.10
) do not depend on
\bm{\tau}
and
\bm{s}
.

Remark C.1
.

An element of
\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
is to be viewed as an assignment of time-dependent vector fields

(-\infty,0]\to\mathfrak{X},\penalty\ [0,\lambda_{1}]\to\mathfrak{X},\penalty\ \dots\penalty\ ,[0,\lambda_{\ell-1}]\to\mathfrak{X},\penalty\ [0,+\infty)\to\mathfrak{X}

to each
(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{\ell-1})\in\mathscr{D}_{\ell-1}
.

Given
\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}=(\bm{Z}^{-},\bm{Z}_{1}^{0},\dots,\bm{Z}_{\ell-1}^{0},\bm{Z}^{+})\in\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
we define
the space of
\ell
-switching data consistent with background data
\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}
:

(C.12)

\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s},\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}})=\mathfrak{X}_{-}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{-})\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{1}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{0}_{1})\times\dots\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{\ell-1}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{0}_{\ell-1})\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{+}).

For later use we also define the following:

(C.13)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{-}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s},\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}})=\mathfrak{X}_{-}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{-})\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{1}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{0}_{1})\times\dots\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{\ell-1}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{0}_{\ell-1}),

(C.14)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{+}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s},\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}})=\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{1}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{0}_{1})\times\dots\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{\ell-1}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{0}_{\ell-1})\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{+}),

(C.15)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{0}(\ell-1,\bm{\tau},\bm{s},\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}})=\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{1}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{0}_{1})\times\dots\times\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{\ell-1}(\ell-1,\bm{Z}^{0}_{\ell-1}).

Similarly, for a given tuple
\bm{Z}=(\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}})_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell)
, we define

(C.16)

\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{Z})=\prod_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s},\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}).

We say that
\bm{Y}^{\ell}=(\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell)
5
5

5

For a while we will make the dependency of
\bm{Y}
and
\bm{Z}
on
\ell
explicit.
is
symmetric

if

(C.17)

\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}=\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}^{\prime}}\text{ for any two orderings }\bm{s},\bm{s}^{\prime},

and
commutative
if

(C.18)

\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}|_{l_{i}=0}=\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau}^{i,1},\bm{s}^{i}}|_{l_{i}=0}=\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau}^{i,2},\bm{s}^{i}}|_{l_{i}=0}\text{ for all }i=1,\dots,\ell-1,

where
\bm{s}^{i}
is obtained from
\bm{s}
by transposing
s_{i}
and
s_{i+1}
and
\bm{\tau}^{i,1}
and
\bm{\tau}^{i,2}
are obtained from
\bm{\tau}
by modifying
\tau_{i}
and
\tau_{i+1}
using the following rules:

(C.19)

\displaystyle\tau_{i}^{i,1}=\tau_{i}^{i,2}=\tau_{i+1}\text{ and }\tau_{i+1}^{i,1}=\tau_{i+1}^{i,2}=\tau_{i}\text{ if }|\tau_{i}\cap\tau_{i+1}|\neq 1;

(C.20)

\displaystyle\text{if }\tau_{i}=\{a,b\}\text{ and }\tau_{i+1}=\{b,c\},\text{ then }\tau_{i}^{i,1}=\{a,c\},\tau_{i+1}^{i,1}=\{a,b\},\tau_{i}^{i,2}=\{b,c\},\tau_{i+1}^{i,2}=\{a,c\}.

(
C.20
) is related to the commutativity property of the switching map as explained in Proposition
2.6
.

As in (
B.19
) we define
\tilde{W}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s},\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}^{\ell}),\bm{\tau},\bm{s})

and let
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
be the fiber over
\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s},\bm{Z}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}})
of the natural projection (note that we are slightly abusing notation here). We say that the perturbation data
\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}
is
regular
if all the spaces
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\bm{N};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
are smooth manifolds for all choices of
\bm{N}
as in Appendix
B
.

Figure C.1
.
On the left we schematically show the domain of an MFLS with
\ell
switchings, with switching markers denoted by crosses. When the
j
th length
l_{j}
goes to
\infty
, we prescribe background data dictated by the data
\bm{Y}^{j}
and
\bm{Y}^{\ell-j}
picked for moduli spaces of MFLS with
j
and
\ell-j
switchings, respectively. Explicitly, for
i>j
we have
Z^{0}_{ij}=Y^{\ell-j}_{i-j}
and for
k<j
we have
Z^{0}_{kj}=Y^{j}_{k}
.

Definition C.2
.

A
universal switching data
is a collection
\bm{Y}=(\bm{Y}^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
of
\ell
-switching data
\bm{Y}^{\ell}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell)
for all integers
\ell\geq 1
such that the following hold:

(1)

For each
\ell
the data
\bm{Y}^{\ell}
is symmetric, commutative and regular.

(2)

The data is
consistent
, i.e.,
\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}^{<\ell})
, where
\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}^{<\ell}
is obtained from the collection
(\bm{Y}^{1},\dots,\bm{Y}^{\ell-1}
) as follows:
Let us write
\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}^{<\ell}=(\bm{Z}^{-},\bm{Z}_{1}^{0},\dots,\bm{Z}_{\ell-1}^{0},\bm{Z}^{+})
, where

\bm{Z}^{\pm}=(Z^{\pm}_{1},\dots,Z^{\pm}_{\ell-1})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\bm{Z}^{0}_{i}=(Z^{0}_{i1},\dots,Z^{0}_{i(i-1)},Z_{ii}^{+},Z_{ii}^{-},Z^{0}_{i(i+1)},\dots,Z^{0}_{i(\ell-1)}).

Then the vector

(Z^{-}_{\ell^{\prime}},Z^{0}_{1\ell^{\prime}},\dots,Z^{0}_{(\ell^{\prime}-1)\ell^{\prime}},Z^{+}_{\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime}},Z^{-}_{\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime}},Z^{0}_{(\ell^{\prime}+1)\ell^{\prime}},\dots,Z^{0}_{(\ell-1)\ell^{\prime}},Z^{+}_{\ell^{\prime}})

is equal to
(\bm{Y}^{\ell^{\prime}},\bm{Y}^{\ell-\ell^{\prime}})
for each
\ell^{\prime}
such that
1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell-1
; see Figure
C.1
.

The following result is a generalization of Theorem
B.6
and asserts the existence of regular
\ell
-switching data consistent with a given background data.

Lemma C.3
.

Given
\bm{Z}^{\ell}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell)
, for a generic
\bm{Y}^{\ell}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{Z}^{\ell})
:

(1)

The spaces
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
are smooth manifolds with corners of dimension

(C.21)

\operatorname{dim}\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})-\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime})-(n-2)\ell-1.

(2)

Given any collection of submanifolds
\bm{N}=(N_{1},\dots,N_{r})
associated with a given tuple
\bm{\tau}
and a partition of
(k_{1},\dots,k_{r})
of
\ell
as in Theorem
B.6
, the space
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\bm{N};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
is a smooth manifold of dimension at most

(C.22)

\operatorname{dim}\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\bm{N};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})-\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime})-(n-1)\ell+r-1,

where
k=\operatorname{max}(k_{1},\dots,k_{r})
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Theorem
B.6
. The main difference is that, for each pair
(\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
, instead of looking for a residual subset of
\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
, we now have to find a residual subset of the closed nonempty affine subset
\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s},\bm{Z}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}^{\ell})\subset\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(\ell,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
. The proof of Theorem
B.6
remains essentially unaffected; see
[
Mes18
, Lemma 5.1]
for more details.
∎

Theorem C.4
.

There exists a universal switching data
\bm{Y}=(\bm{Y}^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
.

Proof.

This is proved by induction. For
\ell=1
, there is no background and we take a regular
\bm{Y}^{1}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(1)=\prod_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\mathfrak{X}_{-}\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}
. For
\ell>1
,
\bm{Z}^{\ell}
is obtained from pairs
(\bm{Y}^{\ell^{\prime}},\bm{Y}^{\ell-\ell^{\prime}})
for all
\ell^{\prime}
such that
1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell-1
, subject to the consistency condition of Definition
C.2
(2). Lemma
C.3
guarantees the existence of a regular
\bm{Z}^{\ell}
.
∎

C.2.
Compactness for MFLS

Theorem C.5
.

Let
\bm{Y}=(\bm{Y}^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
be a choice of universal switching data.

(1)

If
\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime})-\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})-(n-2)\ell-1=0
, then
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
consists of a finite number of points.

(2)

If
\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime})-\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})-(n-2)\ell-1=1
, then
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
admits a compactification
\overline{\mathscr{M}}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
whose boundary is covered by the following strata:

(C.23)

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell^{\prime}}_{\bm{\tau}_{1},\bm{s}_{1}},\bm{\tau}_{1},\bm{s}_{1})\times\mathscr{M}_{\ell-\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime\prime},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell-\ell^{\prime}}_{\bm{\tau}_{2},\bm{s}_{2}},\bm{\tau}_{1},\bm{s}_{2}),\quad 0\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell,

(C.24)

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\Delta^{\tau_{i},\tau_{i+1}}_{I_{s_{i}},I_{s_{i+1}}};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s}),

where
(\bm{\tau}_{1},\bm{\tau}_{2})=\bm{\tau}
with
|\bm{\tau}_{1}|=\ell^{\prime}
;
\bm{s}_{1}
and
\bm{s}_{2}
are permutations of
\{1,\dots,\ell^{\prime}\}
and
\{1,\dots,\ell-\ell^{\prime}\}
, such that for
\bm{s}=(\bm{s}_{1}^{\prime},\bm{s}_{2}^{\prime})
with
|\bm{s}_{1}^{\prime}|=\ell^{\prime}
the ordering of
\bm{s}_{1}
(resp.
\bm{s}_{2}
) coincides with that of
\bm{s}_{1}^{\prime}
(resp.
\bm{s}_{2}^{\prime}
); and
\bm{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}
is a critical point such that

\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})-\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime\prime})-(n-2)\ell^{\prime}-1=\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime\prime})-\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime})-(n-2)(\ell-\ell^{\prime})-1=0.

Proof.

If a sequence
(\bm{\Gamma}^{j})_{j\geq 1}
of MFLS’s in
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
does not converge to an MFLS in this space, then either one of the finite trajectories
\bm{\Gamma}^{j}_{i}
converges to a pair of semi-infinite trajectories or a semi-infinite trajectory converges to a pair consisting of an infinite trajectory and a semi-infinite trajectory; this corresponds to
l_{i}\to\infty
for some
i
such that
1\leq i\leq\ell
and is described by (
C.23
). Indeed the convergence takes place by
[
AM06
, Theorem 3.5]
, since
X
is a pseudogradient and
\bm{Y}=X
on the regions of the MFLS that are far away from the switches. In the case of (1), such convergence does not occur by regularity and index considerations; in the case of (2) such convergence is possible.

The other possibility is that
l_{i}\to 0
for at least one index
i
satisfying
1\leq i\leq\ell-1
. If there is only one such index, then the limit
\bm{\Gamma}^{\infty}
is in (
C.24
) (see Figure
C.2
), unless
\theta_{s_{i}}=\theta_{s_{i+1}}
. The latter does not occur because such a stratum has dimension
-1
; hence by regularity the stratum must be empty. On the other hand, if more than one
l_{i}
converges to
0
, then such
\bm{\Gamma}^{\infty}
belongs to a manifold of type
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\bm{N};\bm{Y},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
, which has negative dimension by (
C.22
); hence such
\bm{\Gamma}^{\infty}
cannot be in the limit.
∎

Remark C.6
.

Observe that Theorem
C.5
(2) is not entirely accurate, because for moduli spaces of MFLS with
\ell
switchings we used switching maps that depend on all
\ell
time coordinates
\theta
and even when the trajectory breaks those switching maps still depend on all
\ell
coordinates, although we claim that it would depend on a fewer number of coordinates. To fix this issue one should also make switching maps depend on length coordinates
l_{i}
, in particular on distances towards nearest switchings along the trajectory. We leave this to the attentive reader.

Corollary C.7
.

If
\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma}^{\prime})-\operatorname{ind}(\bm{\gamma})-(n-2)\ell-1=1
, then

\bigcup_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})

admits a compactification with boundary consisting only of strata of the form (
C.23
).

Proof.

The main observation here is that for any tuple
\bm{\tau}
and any ordering
\bm{s}
, every point of

\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\Delta^{\tau_{i},\tau_{i+1}}_{I_{s_{i}},I_{s_{i+1}}};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})

is also a point of

\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},\Delta^{\tau^{i,j}_{i},\tau^{i,j}_{i+1}}_{I_{s^{i}_{i}},I_{s^{i}_{i+1}}};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau}^{i,j},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau}^{i,j},\bm{s}^{i}),

where
j=1,2
, depending on whether
\theta_{s_{i}}
is greater than
\theta_{s_{i+1}}
; for the notation see (
C.20
). This holds, since the data
\bm{Y}
is commutative and the switching maps satisfy the commutativity relations by Proposition
2.19
, which allows us to switch
s_{i}
and
s_{i+1}
. Note that by definition the switching maps associated to the remaining
\theta
-coordinates do not change. Therefore, we may identify these points of compactifications
\overline{\mathscr{M}}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
and make them into interior points of the union of such compactifications, while respecting the orientations. This last observation is clearly seen from our orientation conventions since essentially while switching the order of
s_{i}
and
s_{i+1}
in our convention (
2.39
) on the orientations of these spaces we only change the order of two length coordinates
l_{i}
and
l_{i+1}
.
∎

[formula]
[formula]

Figure C.2
.
Here we schematically depict portions of MFLS in
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime};\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
perturbed using
\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}
. The dot in the middle is a zero-length trajectory that passes through the deeper stratum
\Delta^{\tau_{i},\tau_{i+1}}_{I_{s_{i}},I_{s_{i+1}}}
.

C.3.
Compactness for MFTS

We introduce the various parameter spaces that ensure the compatibility on the boundary of moduli spaces of MFTS. Given
(T,\bm{\tau})\in\mathscr{T}^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}_{d}
and
\bm{s}\in S(T,\bm{\tau})
we define:

(C.25)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})=\prod_{i=1}^{d}\Big(\mathfrak{X}_{-}(|E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)|+\ell)\times\prod_{j=1}^{\ell_{e_{i}}}\mathfrak{X}_{0}(|E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)|+\ell)\Big)\times\prod_{e\in E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)}\prod_{j=0}^{\ell_{e}}\mathfrak{X}_{0}(|E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)|+\ell)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{j=1}^{\ell_{e_{0}}}\mathfrak{X}_{0}(|E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)|+\ell)\times\mathfrak{X}_{-}(|E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)|+\ell).

For simplicity we have suppressed the superscripts
j
in summands of the form
\mathfrak{X}_{0}^{j}(k)
; compare with (
C.8
).
The three groups of factors of
\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
correspond to the incoming edges
e_{1},\dots,e_{d}
, the interior edges
E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)
, and the outgoing edge
e_{0}
. Observe that the right-hand side of (
C.25
) does not depend on
\ell^{\prime}
.
We write
\bm{\tau}_{e}
for the restriction of
\bm{\tau}
to
e\in E(T)
and
\bm{s}_{e}
for the ordering of the switches on
e
induced by
\bm{s}
.

The space
\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}^{\operatorname{back}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
is defined using (
C.25
) by adding superscripts “back” to each factor to indicate background perturbation spaces; these background perturbation spaces are defined in a manner analogous to (
C.4
) and (
C.6
). Given an element
\bm{Z}=((\bm{Z}_{\tau_{e},\bm{s}_{e}})_{e\in E(T)})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}^{\operatorname{back}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
, we define the subspace
\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s},\bm{Z})
of
\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
to be equal to

\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathfrak{X}_{-}(\ell_{e_{i}},\bm{\tau}_{e_{i}},\bm{s}_{e_{i}},\bm{Z}_{\tau_{e_{i}},\bm{s}_{e_{i}}})\times\prod_{e\in E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)}\mathfrak{X}_{0}(\ell_{e},\bm{\tau}_{e},\bm{s}_{e},\bm{Z}_{\tau_{e},\bm{s}_{e}})\times\mathfrak{X}_{+}(\ell_{e_{0}},\bm{\tau}_{e_{0}},\bm{s}_{e_{0}},\bm{Z}_{\tau_{e_{0}},\bm{s}_{e_{0}}}),

where the notation that we use here is analogous to (
C.13
)–(
C.15
).

Given a collection of edges
F\subset E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)
, there is a tree with switching data
(T/F,\bm{\tau}^{\prime})
, obtained from
(T,\bm{\tau})
by contracting all the edges in
F
, following the prescription given in Section
2.4
. Moreover, given
\bm{\tau}
and
\bm{s}\in S(T,\bm{\tau})
, there are induced
\bm{\tau}_{T/F}
and
\bm{s}_{T/F}\in S(T/F,\bm{\tau}_{T/F})
. There exist forgetful maps

(C.26)

\displaystyle\pi_{F}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})\to\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(T/F,\bm{\tau}_{T/F},\bm{s}_{T/F}),\quad\pi_{F}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}^{\operatorname{back}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})\to\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}^{\operatorname{back}}(T/F,\bm{\tau}_{T/F},\bm{s}_{T/F}),

which simply “forget” the coordinates corresponding to contracted edges; see
[
Mes18
, Definition 4.13]
for more details.

Given
d\geq 2
, a
d
-perturbation data
\bm{Y}^{d}
is a collection of
\bm{Y}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
for all
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
(T,\bm{\tau})\in\mathscr{T}_{d}^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
, and
\bm{s}\in(T,\bm{\tau})
. We say that
\bm{Y}^{d}
is
regular
if all the spaces

\mathscr{M}_{T}(\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\bm{\gamma}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\bm{\gamma}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\bm{\gamma}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\bm{\gamma}\hfil$\crcr}}},\bm{\gamma}_{0},(\bm{N}_{e})_{e\in E(T)},(\bm{N}_{v})_{v\in V_{\operatorname{int}}(T)};\bm{Y}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})

are transversely cut out smooth manifolds;
symmetric
if

(C.27)

\bm{Y}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}=\bm{Y}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}^{\prime}}

for any
(T,\bm{\tau})\in\mathscr{T}_{d}^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
and
\bm{s},\bm{s}^{\prime}\in S(T,\bm{\tau})
; and
commutative
if all the relations akin to (
C.18
) corresponding to “collapsing” two interior markers are satisfied.

Given
d\geq 2
, a
background
d
-perturbation data
\bm{Z}^{d}
is a collection
\bm{Z}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{\operatorname{sw}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
for all
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
(T,\bm{\tau})\in\mathscr{T}_{d}^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
with at least one finite-length segment (i.e.,
|E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)|+\ell>0
), and
\bm{s}\in S(T,\bm{\tau})
. Here a
segment
is (the closure of) a component of an edge cut along the switching locus. The data
\bm{Z}^{d}
is
admissible
if
\pi_{F}(\bm{Z}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}})=\bm{Z}_{T/F,\bm{\tau}_{T/F},\bm{s}_{T/F}}
for all
(T,\bm{\tau})\in\mathscr{T}_{d}^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
,
\bm{s}\in S(T,\bm{\tau})
, and
F\subset E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)
.

Given a tuple
\bm{Y}^{<d}:=(\bm{Y}^{2},\dots,\bm{Y}^{d-1})
, we may associate an admissible background
d
-perturbation data
\bm{Z}^{<d}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{\operatorname{sw}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
for each
(T,\bm{\tau})\in\mathscr{T}_{d}^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
and each
\bm{s}
. By cutting the
i
th segment of an edge
e\in E(T)
into two half-infinite segments, one splits
(T,\bm{\tau})
into
(T_{1}^{e,i},\bm{\tau}_{T_{1}})\in\mathscr{T}_{k}^{\ell_{1},\ell^{\prime}_{1}}
and
(T_{2}^{e,i},\bm{\tau}_{T_{2}})\in\mathscr{T}_{d-k+1}^{\ell-\ell_{1},\ell^{\prime}-\ell^{\prime}_{1}}
, and we simply use
\bm{Y}_{T^{e,i}_{1},\bm{\tau}_{T_{1}},\bm{s}_{T_{1}}}
and
\bm{Y}_{T^{e,i}_{2},\bm{\tau}_{T_{2}},\bm{s}_{T_{2}}}
to assign such background data; see the discussion prior to
[
Mes18
, Definition 5.15]
for more details. Here
\bm{\tau}_{T_{1}}
is the restriction of
\bm{\tau}
to
T_{1}^{e,i}
and
\bm{s}_{T_{1}}
is the ordering of the
\ell_{1}+\ell_{1}^{\prime}
switches of
T_{1}^{e,i}
induced from
\bm{s}
.

Definition C.8
.

(1)

A tree perturbation data is a family
\bm{Y}=(\bm{Y}^{d})_{d\geq 2}
of
d
-perturbation data
\bm{Y}^{d}
. The data
\bm{Y}
is regular (resp. symmetric, commutative) if each
\bm{Y}^{d}
is regular (resp. symmetric, commutative).

(2)

A tree perturbation data
\bm{Y}=(\bm{Y}^{d})_{d\geq 2}
is consistent if for all
d\geq 2
,
(T,\bm{\tau})\in\mathscr{T}^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}_{d}
, and
\bm{s}\in S(T,\bm{\tau})
,
\bm{Y}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{sw}}(T,\bm{Z}^{<d}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}})
, where
\bm{Z}^{<d}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\operatorname{back}}_{\operatorname{sw}}(T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
is the background
d
-perturbation associated to
\bm{Y}^{<d}
as described above.

(3)

A tree perturbation data
\bm{Y}=(\bm{Y}^{d})_{d\geq 2}
is universal if it is symmetric, commutative, regular, consistent, and the following hold:

(a)

for all
(T,\bm{\tau})\in\mathscr{T}_{d}^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
,
\bm{s}\in S(T,\bm{\tau})
, and
F\subset E_{\operatorname{int}}(T)

(C.28)

\pi_{F}(\bm{Y}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}})=\bm{Y}_{{T/F},\bm{\tau}_{T/F},\bm{s}_{T/F}};

(b)

for all
v\in V_{\operatorname{int}}(T)
and
i>0
, if
\bm{\tau}^{\prime}
is obtained from
\bm{\tau}
by moving the last term of
\bm{\tau}_{e^{v}_{i}}
to the first term of
\bm{\tau}_{v}
(see Definitions
2.17
and
2.18
for the notation) and
\bm{s}^{\prime}\in S(T,\bm{\tau}^{\prime})
is the induced ordering, then

(C.29)

\bm{Y}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}|_{\{l^{e_{i}^{v}}_{\ell_{e_{i}^{v}}}=0\}}=\bm{Y}_{T,\bm{\tau}^{\prime},\bm{s}^{\prime}};

(c)

for all
v\in V_{\operatorname{int}}(T)
, if
\bm{\tau}^{\prime}
is obtained from
\bm{\tau}
by moving the first term of
\bm{\tau}_{e^{v}_{0}}
to the last term of
\bm{\tau}_{v}
and
\bm{s}^{\prime}\in S(T,\bm{\tau}^{\prime})
is the induced ordering, then

(C.30)

\bm{Y}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}}|_{\{l^{e_{0}^{v}}_{\ell_{e_{0}^{v}}}=0\}}=\bm{Y}_{T,\bm{s}^{\prime},\bm{\tau}^{\prime}}.

.

2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\bm{\gamma}\hfil$\crcr}}},\bm{\gamma}_{0};\bm{Y}_{T,\bm{\tau},\bm{s}},\bm{\tau},\bm{s})
covered by the following strata:

in (
C.9
) is given by Definition (
C.8
)(c)(2) or (3).
Moreover, the points of (
C.9
) are interior points of the compactification.

Proof.

The existence of a universal tree perturbation data is proved by an induction similar to the proof of Theorem
C.5
(1) and
[
Mes18
, Lemma 5.16]
and we leave details to the reader.

First, if one of the segments is “stretched”, then the sequence of such trajectories approaches an element of (
C.31
) as in the proof of Theorem
C.5
(3). Note that if two or more such segments are “stretched”, the limit belongs to a moduli space of negative dimension by the regularity and consistency of the data
\bm{Y}
, so this does not occur.

Next suppose that the length of a finite-length segment
a
converges to zero. By dimension reasons only one segment
a
has length that goes to zero. If no endpoint of
a
is in
V_{\operatorname{int}}(T)
, then we have the boundary stratum (
C.9
) and the analysis of this case is completely analogous to that of (
C.24
) in Theorem
C.5
. If both endpoints of
a
are in
V_{\operatorname{int}}(T)
, i.e.,
a=e\in E(T)
, then we are in the case of (
C.9
). The analysis of this is similar to the finite-dimensional case; see
[
Mes18
, Theorem 5.17]
.

Finally assume that exactly one endpoint of
a
is in
V_{\operatorname{int}}(T)
. Let
v
be such a vertex and let
a\subset e_{i}^{v}
. If
i>0
(resp.
i=0
), then the limit MFTS belongs to
\mathscr{M}_{T}(\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\bm{\gamma}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern
